THE MIGRATIONS GO THROUGH `d1 migrations apply`, whose `d1_migrations` ledger lives in the database, which is the change that was actually load-bearing: ACC1c could only WARN that 0002 is not idempotent and then trust a reader to apply each file once, and "once each, in order" was never a law, it was a hope addressed to whoever read the comment. THE SIGNING PAIR IS MINTED ONLY IF ABSENT - re-minting invalidates every token in flight - and both halves are piped straight into `wrangler secret put`, never echoed, filed or put on a command line. AND THE SERVICE PUBLISHES ITS OWN PUBLIC HALF at `/v1/pubkey`, openly and before any credential, because a public key can verify and cannot mint: the old step 3 ended "...and the PUBLIC half into the relay's config", a copy-paste between two systems done by a person once at a moment nobody records, and a pair minted where nobody watches would otherwise be a verifying key nobody can ever read again. AND THE FIRST THING IT HAD TO LEARN IS THAT WE ALREADY HAVE ACCOUNTS: SOC1 built them five days ago and no page called them that - `dagger.online.account` and its secret in appStorage, `acct:<id>` and `asecret:<id>` in the hub's Durable Object storage, with friends, requests and the four-seat party already hanging off the id. So the arc is not "make accounts exist"; it is give the account that exists a way to be PROVEN (a provider link, so it survives a cleared browser and reaches a second device) and somewhere to KEEP THINGS (the saves). THE WALL IS AT CLOUD SAVES AND NOWHERE ELSE, which is Mac's own cut and is better than the two this session argued for first: a guest connects, is seen, walks and CHATS, under a generated name it cannot choose; a linked account gets its own name and its saves. So THE ONLY PEOPLE WHO CAN TAKE A NAME ARE THE PEOPLE WHO CAN BE BANNED - the impersonation vector and the accountability arrive together, where "accounts required for online" buys moderation by closing the door that makes online worth opening and "random names for everyone" leaves linking with nothing to offer. D1 OWNS IDENTITY and the hub keeps what it keeps, for one reason that is not taste: a provider link is a lookup by somebody else's id, and a Durable Object cannot index across all players without becoming a global object every sign-in queues behind. THE TOKEN IS THE REAL NEW SEAM - today the hello carries a client-asserted `name` the relay only sanitises, which is fine while a name means nothing and a hole worth exploiting the moment it means "this is a linked account"; the account service signs a short-lived token, the relay verifies it and reads the name OUT of it, one mechanism covering guests and linked players alike, and the relay never touches D1. TWO WORKERS, settled by SLAM8: a relay deploy drops every connected player, and the bundle is hashed by its RAW BYTES, so accounts in that bundle would mean every auth tweak drops everyone mid-dungeon (DEPLOY-PROSE abandoned a one-comment fix for exactly this the same day). R2 FOR THE BLOB, D1 FOR THE CARD, screenshot its own object; and THE CLOUD IS A BACKUP, THE LOCAL SAVE STAYS AUTHORITATIVE - this repo has CHARID1 and SP1 because players lost games, and SP1's carrier already moves a slot between two stores, so the cloud is a third destination rather than a new kind of save. From `Lattymoy/fight-life-source` @ 49f4ff4: `fight-life-source/server/src/auth.js` whole (provider-agnostic over an opaque {providerId, email}), the `account_links`/`sessions`/`player_state` migrations, fight-life-source/src/online/playerState.js's hydrated mirror (synchronous reads matter here - the save callers are synchronous and some are frame-timed), and its nameGen.js; NOT the ladder, Elo, economy, pass or verification stack. Two places its schema is not enough: `handle` has no uniqueness constraint (here the name IS the identity, so `handle_lc` unique), and `player_state` is sized for 96 KB blobs. The existing SOC ids are ADOPTED rather than replaced, so friend lists survive. Open: whether the hub leaves the relay bundle, rename cost, the guest name's persistence, and where the desktop app mints a token. **The Cloudflare API token is not being rotated** - Mac's call, made with the exposure in front of him ("Im not rotating. Lets do this"), and this arc puts D1 and R2 on that same account. The standing law is unchanged and pinned: it lives in GitHub Actions secrets, never in a file and never on a command line. AUDIT-ACC (Mac: "Let's do a proper audit on everything") FOUND THE WORKER COULD NOT BOOT. A module Worker reads every named export of its entrypoint as an ENTRYPOINT, so workerd refused to start over `export const ACCOUNT_VERSION` - and no node test could have caught it, because test/accountworker.test.js IMPORTED those very names and so proved they existed while the runtime rejected them for existing. Importability is not deployability. The relay is the control: server/src/index.js exports `default` and the `Room` Durable Object CLASS and has deployed for months, so the law is no named VALUE exports. Fixed by giving the constants and the key cache their own homes (service.js, signing.js). THE SECOND FINDING WAS THE RE-MINT GUARD FAILING OPEN: `wrangler secret list` has no `--json` flag, so wrangler printed HELP TEXT and exited 0, jq could not parse it, the guard went false, and every deploy would have minted a new signing pair and signed out everybody online. The flag was the trigger; the `|| echo '[]'` fallback was the fault - a destructive default reached by failing to read something. Both were found by STANDING THE SERVICE UP (tools/accountProbe.mjs, `npm run account`, 21 checks in a real workerd), which also measured what the arc had only assumed: Ed25519 exists in that runtime, PBKDF2 at 210k costs 36ms there, both migrations apply in order through wrangler's ledger, and a token the Worker SIGNS verifies against the key the Worker PUBLISHES. AND NOTHING IN THIS ARC IS IN THE RELAY BUNDLE - RELAY_GRAPH is five files and none is identityToken.js or under server-account/, so the two-Worker split's promise that this deploy drops nobody is measured rather than argued; ACC1d changes that the moment the relay imports the token module. Four smaller findings: a harness error that was NOT a defect (checked before reporting), the player id under two names (`id` vs `playerId` in one API), `.wrangler/` and `.dev.vars` unignored, and this arc's record under-crediting Fight Life - it said "that bug is not being ported" when they had hit it, fixed it with a sessions table, and this is their fix carried over. AUDIT-ACC PART TWO (Mac: "This is it?", then "Take your time") took the four lenses the first pass had skipped - the token read adversarially, the port read against Fight Life member by member, the credential paths read for security, and DEPLOY-PROSE, which had been audited AROUND but never itself. EIGHT MORE FINDINGS, FIVE LIVE. F7 THE BANK COULD SPELL A NAME THE WIRE COULD NOT CARRY: NAME_MAX is 24 and four of the 173,330 names the generator can produce are 25, so createGuest threw and /v1/auth/guest answered 500 for about one guest in 43,000 - and the comment there said it "should never fire" because nobody had multiplied the bank out. It survived ACC1b because that pin SAMPLED eighteen draws and four in 173,330 is invisible to a sample; the new pin ENUMERATES the space. Raising NAME_MAX was not available (it is in the relay bundle, so it drops every player), so the generator rejects those draws - the same rejection sampling `pick` uses one function up. F8 THE TOKEN IS A BEARER CREDENTIAL AND NOTHING ANYWHERE CONSIDERED REPLAY - it closes forgery, not replay, and one-shot at the relay is the cheap answer; written into identityToken.js now, while that file is still outside the bundle and editing it is free. F9 A SESSION NEVER EXPIRED - SESSION_IDLE_S was declared, documented and enforced by nothing; Fight Life enforces it on the auth path and DELETES the row, which needs no cron that can stop running. F10 an unguarded cosmetic last_seen write turned an ALREADY AUTHORISED request into a 500. F12 ONLY STRANGERS WERE RATE-LIMITED - everything behind a session was unbounded, so one secret could mint signatures without limit; bounded per account now, and 429 rather than 401 because telling a rate-limited player their credentials are wrong sends them to reset a password that was never the problem. F13 THE SESSION SECRET RODE IN A URL on /v1/account, excused as "read-only" - which answers the wrong risk, because a URL lands in Cloudflare's logs, in a Referer and in browser history; it is an Authorization header now and the old door is pinned SHUT rather than merely unused. F14 a claim that lived only in the PR body, which is the one place this project does not gate: DEPLOY-PROSE said the deploy had been automatic "for fifteen weeks" when relay-deploy.yml landed 2026-09-16, five days before. DEPLOY-PROSE is otherwise sound - the corrections are true, the gate derives, and the wire.js exclusion's premise still stands. AUDIT-ACC PART THREE (Mac: "Before we merge. This is it?") read the one thing that runs at MERGE TIME - the deploy workflow, which had never executed - against wrangler's own bundle, and found the worst finding of the audit. F15 THE FIRST RUN COULD NOT HAVE SUCCEEDED: `wrangler secret list` does not answer empty on a Worker that does not exist, it FAILS ("Worker not found. If this is a new Worker, run `wrangler deploy` first"), which on a first deploy is the expected state - so the minting step aborted the job before anything was ever deployed. F1 is what made it fatal: before F1 the step swallowed the failure, and F1 correctly made an unreadable listing stop the job, turning a first-run certainty into a hard stop. BOTH HALVES WERE RIGHT AND THE ORDER WAS WRONG, so the pin holds an ORDER and names the keyless-service arm that makes deploying before the key safe. F16 the verify steps built their host from the worker name (derived) plus a typed account subdomain - a second home for a fact, the SLAM13 shape - so the deploy publishes the URL wrangler says it landed on and no workers.dev host appears in any live line, which also makes the workflow portable. Verified rather than assumed, out of wrangler's bundle: d1 list --json prints raw records with the banner suppressed, secret list --format json prints [{name,type}], and secret put reads stdin whenever stdin is not a TTY - so the pipe that keeps the private half out of every log is sound. AND IT STOOD ITSELF UP ON THE FIRST RUN (2026-09-21, merge 6743d3bdb): the workflow created the D1 database, resolved its id, applied 0001 and 0002 through the ledger, deployed to https://daggerfall-accounts.mackcothran.workers.dev, reported "no signing key yet - minting one" and put both halves on the Worker, then verified /v1/health serves acct1 and /v1/pubkey hands back a 43-character key. No person touched a Cloudflare dashboard at any point. THE SERVICE IS LIVE. The relay deploy on the same commit completed having deployed NOTHING - RELAY_VERSION was unchanged, so it stopped at the /health curl and nobody was dropped, which is the two-Worker split's central claim demonstrated in production rather than argued. AUDIT-ACC F8 IS NOW SETTLED (Mac, asked whether to close replay or accept the five-minute window: "Yes"): A TOKEN IS SPENT ONCE - the relay keeps the signatures it has verified and refuses a repeat, and the TTL ceiling belongs in the relay's config beside the public key rather than at a call site. Nothing enforces either yet, because the refusal lives in the relay and the relay does not import the token module; test/identitytoken.test.js holds the decision against RELAY_GRAPH and FAILS THE DAY ACC1d puts that module in the bundle, so the decision cannot be lost between now and then. OPEN: ACC1d (the client asks for a token, the relay verifies it against the published key - it costs a RELAY_VERSION bump and drops every connected player), ACC2 (the saves). STILL UNEXAMINED and named as such: server-account/src/password.js never got the adversarial read the token got, and tools/accountProbe.mjs has never been mutated. ACC1e THE ACCOUNT CREATION SCREEN (Mac: "Yes, please be detailed and match the enhanced aesthetic"): the card at the head of the Online pane, gating nothing - ACC0's wall is at cloud saves and every button below it works with no account. It went before ACC1d because it is FREE - it touches nothing in the relay bundle, so no RELAY_VERSION bump and nobody is dropped, and spending that drop once AFTER the screen exists means one deploy delivers something a player can see. The flow thinks (ui/accountFlow.js, node-drivable) and the card draws (ui/enhancedAccount.js), which is ChargenFlow/enhancedChargen again. ONE PRESS IS ONE ACCOUNT: registering is two calls and a press between them opens a second guest row nothing will ever adopt. A dead credential signs the device out and a BLIP does not. The recovery code is a STAGE - email is optional so it IS the reset - never stored, and the only way off it is acknowledging it. ACC1e F1: `.fieldlabel` read var(--ash) and NOTHING has ever declared --ash, so every field label in the enhanced skin has been inheriting --bone since the rule was written; found by standing the card up in a real Chromium and reading the COMPUTED colour, which no source sweep can do. The two name shapes moved to src/net/handleShape.js so the client could ask the same question without the only backwards import in the repo, both halves together because ACC1b's own comment says neither may drift from the other. 12 mutants, 12 dead, 0 survived - one survivor on the first run was the no-trim password law, stated in a comment and asked by nothing. ACC1f MOVED IT TO THE FRONT DOOR (Mac: the Online pane is "reserved for a detailed tile based design for your saves", and "the online details itself will live as a popup on main menu startup and a new profile icon"): the card leaves the pane for a window over the pixel home, offered ONCE per visit to a device with nobody signed in, plus a profile mark top-right that reopens it. An offer and not a gate - ACC0's wall is at cloud saves and every door on the screen works without an account - and it closes on Escape, on Close, and on a tap outside like the pause window. THREE HOST FAULTS THE SCREENSHOT FOUND AND NO TEST WOULD HAVE: no scrim, so the door's own menu read through the window; the heading in Cormorant inside a pixel window, because `.shell .card h3` forces the pixel face and the door is .px-home rather than .shell; and the card's buttons lowercase beside a spaced CLOSE for the same reason on `.shell .act`. Both rules widened to the door's window rather than copied. And outsideTap.test.js's claim that the front door has no scrim had become false, so it names BOTH wirings now and checks the account one is guarded by accountOpen. ACC1d CLOSED THE HOLE ACC1a OPENED THE ARC TO CLOSE (2026-09-22, Mac: "Go in order. Take your time"): the client mints a short-lived token per CONNECTION from the account session this device holds, the hello carries it as `tok`, wire.js checks its SHAPE only (it is sync and pure and must stay that way - a verifier needs WebCrypto, a key and an await), and the relay VERIFIES it and takes the name OUT of the token instead of off the frame. THE TOKEN IS NOT REQUIRED, and that is the decision rather than an omission: required would make the relay unusable whenever the account service is down, which is the second-Worker coupling ACC0 split to avoid; optional-and-name-from-the-token-when-present buys nothing, because a forger just omits it. So an unverified hello is admitted exactly as every build before this slice was, and the relay says which names it VOUCHES FOR - with the honest limit written down rather than discovered: a token makes a name TRUSTWORTHY, it does not yet make one MANDATORY, and ACC0's wall is not fully standing until that flip, which is its own slice with its own deploy. A TOKEN IS SPENT ONCE (F8, settled by Mac): the room keeps the signatures it has verified, bounded and swept by the token's own `e`, which closes replay INTO THE SAME ROOM - where the victim is - and does not close replay into a different room, because that needs shared state on the hello path and saying so is better than a comment claiming otherwise. THE PUBLIC KEY AND THE TTL CEILING ARE CONFIG BESIDE EACH OTHER in server/wrangler.toml, as vars, because a public key can verify and cannot mint and fetching it at runtime would put the account service in the relay's startup path - and BOTH DEPLOYS NOW CHECK THE TWO COPIES AGAINST EACH OTHER, since a stale copy fails in the worst way there is: every hello refused as a bad signature with nothing saying why. An unreachable service warns; a real disagreement stops the deploy. THE CLIENT NEVER PAYS FOR A BAD MINUTE AT THE ACCOUNT SERVICE: the minter answers null for no session, a 503, a rate limit or a network failure, TOKEN_WAIT_MS is the whole budget, and a hello with no token is the connection every build before this one made. THE FIRST CUT HAD A REAL HOLE AND THE PINS FOUND IT: `rosterFor` did not carry `v`, so everyone who joined AFTER you would have been marked and everyone already standing in the room would not - a signal true half the time, fixable only by a SECOND relay deploy. D5 states what is deliberately NOT done: the verdict travels to the peer, the chat line and the log, and no mark is DRAWN, because that is a design on a surface Mac reviews and carrying the flag now makes that slice a client build rather than another drop. RELAY_VERSION world84 -> world85, so the merge DROPS EVERY CONNECTED PLAYER; that is the price this arc has been saving since ACC0 chose two Workers. Mutants: tools/mutants/acc1d.json, 22, 22 dead and 0 survived. AUDIT-312 (Mac: "Let's audit this") took the four slices AUDIT-ACC had not seen - ACC1d, ACC2a, ACC2b and TILE1/TILE2 - and wrote TWELVE ADVERSARIAL MUTANTS against laws they claim; FOUR SURVIVED. The relay's token seam held every one (a relay that vouches for names it cannot verify, a config var loosening the module's hard TTL ceiling, a spend-set swept empty before it is asked, a `tok: null` in the hello that would have cost every signed-out player the room), and so did the save service's isolation. WHAT DID NOT HOLD WAS EVERYTHING BETWEEN THE TWO. **F1 THE DELETE HAD NO DOOR**: the route existed, `removeCloudSlot` existed, nothing called either - while the refusal at SAVES_MAX already told a player "delete a save there to make room", an instruction the game gave them no way to follow, on an account that could then never back up again. The tile carries `Delete backup` now (that word, because the tile already has the pane's own Delete one row down - two buttons reading `Delete`, one destroying the game and one destroying the copy, is the worst label this menu could carry), it asks twice, and it never touches this device's store. **F2 `no-character` WAS A SENTENCE NO SURFACE COULD SHOW**: a pre-CHARID1 card fell to `off`, which draws no cloud line at all, so it sat in the list with no button and no reason beside tiles that had one; a sixth state, `wait`, carries the (now shorter) sentence and no button. **F3 THE MENU'S CLOUD ARITHMETIC WAS WHERE NO PIN COULD REACH IT** - `ui/enhancedMenu.js` is DOM and a boot, and three mutants of what a tile SAYS about a backup went through the whole suite untouched: an upload that died between the row and the blob reading as "Backed up", a listing never re-asked after a push, and a slot key without the character half, which makes every character's QuickSave one slot. The decision is `cloudStateOf` in ui/saveTile.js (pure, answering a refusal WORD rather than a sentence) and `slotKeyOf` in systems/cloudSaves.js; the menu keeps the handlers. **F4 `tools/accountProbe.mjs` NEVER SAW ACC2** - the tool that exists because importability is not deployability never touched the new R2 binding or the seven routes, and a binding that is absent does not crash: it answers `no-storage`, which reaches a player as "Cloud saves are unavailable right now" with a green suite behind it. It drives a whole save round trip in workerd's own R2 now - and it turned out to be correct only ONCE, because `wrangler --local` reuses its state, so run two registered a username run one had taken; it stands on a fresh `--persist-to` directory and is 36/36 twice in a row. **F5 THE EXTRACTION CHANGED THE TEN HEADS' CONTRACT**: `loadFaceCanvases` ended `.filter(Boolean)`, which COMPACTS, while `faceIndex` addresses a RECORD NUMBER and `ui/chargenArt.js`'s loadFaceSet - the other reader of the same ten records - never compacts. Recorded rather than paid: the spent-set's eviction comment claims more than it holds (the real bound is the 300s TTL plus the mint rate), and the cloud latch was per page load rather than per visit, which a fresh mount now clears. Mutants: tools/mutants/audit312.json, 10, 9 dead and 1 recorded equivalent. AUDIT-PW (Mac: "Can you read those") CLOSED THE TWO THINGS TWO AUDITS HAD NAMED AS UNEXAMINED AND NEITHER HAD COME BACK TO. **P1: NOTHING MAY HASH AN EMPTY CREDENTIAL.** `codeForHashing` has TWO CONTRACTS - at the mint a null is impossible, at the check a null is the ordinary answer to a typo - and nothing enforced the first; `normalise` answers '' for a null, so a minted code that failed to canonicalise hashed the EMPTY STRING into `recovery_hash`, and `recover`'s own `canon ?? ''` then opens that account with ANY string that is not a code. IT HAS HAPPENED ONCE - password.js's own note records the Q fold doing exactly that to `7GEPQ-47BS9-AYK70-QMWYW`, and the only thing that caught it was a test over minted codes; nothing in the code refused to store the result, so the shape would return with the next alphabet change. The refusal is in `hashPassword`, the one chokepoint every stored credential goes through, and it THROWS - a caller that hands it nothing is a programming error and the router turns it into a logged 500 that stores nothing. **P2: THE CONSTANT-TIME COMPARE FAILED OPEN** - `timingSafeEqual` coerced anything that was not a Uint8Array to an EMPTY one, so two lengths of 0 XOR'd to 0, the loop never ran, and `timingSafeEqual(null, null)` answered TRUE. Unreachable today, which is exactly why it could sit there: a defensive default defending in the wrong direction is worse than none, because it reads as care. AND THE CAMPAIGN FOUND THE PIN'S OWN BLIND SPOT - the loop reads `x[i % x.length]` so a short array that REPEATS into a longer one matches it byte for byte, and the length XOR is the only thing that says no; a prefix dies without it and a repeat does not, and the pin only tested a prefix. What held: PBKDF2-SHA256 at 210k with a per-account salt and a self-describing stored form, NFKC before the KDF, the rehash-on-correct-login upgrade, `verifyPassword` deriving BEFORE it checks whether the row parsed, `parseStored` refusing rather than throwing, and the code alphabet a power of two with a throw if it stops being one. Mutants: tools/mutants/auditpw.json, 6, 6 dead (one survived the first run - the repeat case). AND THE PROBE HAS BEEN MUTATED AT LAST: the question a campaign asks of a PROBE is the inverse of the usual one - break the SERVICE, and does the probe go red? All four do, and TWO THE SUITE CANNOT SEE AT ALL - a named value export back on the entrypoint (the original bug: workerd refuses to start over a constant while the suite stays green BECAUSE it imports those very names) and the R2 binding renamed in wrangler.toml (the suite hands the Worker its own env and never reads that file). Mutants: tools/mutants/acctprobe.json, 4, 4 dead. ACC1d-MARK PAID OFF THE ONE THING ACC1d NAMED AND DID NOT DO (Mac, asked where the verdict should be drawn: "Should be over the head in online how it currently works"): D5 had carried `v` all the way to the client and drawn nothing, deliberately, so that the day a mark existed would be a CLIENT build and not another relay deploy - and that is what it cost, no RELAY_VERSION bump and nobody dropped. THE POLARITY IS MAC'S AND THE FIRST CUT HAD IT BACKWARDS. It put a `?` on the names the relay could NOT vouch for, reasoning that the useful signal is the missing one, and he asked the question that takes that apart: "Why a question mark since even guests get a name?" THE VERDICT DOES NOT DIVIDE GUEST FROM ACCOUNT - a guest with a session mints a token like anybody else and the relay vouches for the name inside it. It divides A NAME THE PLAYER TYPED from A NAME THE SERVICE ISSUED: with no session the hello carries whatever stands in `onlineName` (enhancedMenu.js's "Name over your head"), which the relay only sanitises, so it can be anybody's - the impersonation hole ACC1a opened this arc to close; with a session, guest or linked alike, the service signs a token and the relay takes the name OUT of it. So the badge goes on the name that was CHECKED, for three reasons and the third settles it: it can only ever appear where the service issued the name, so it never becomes wallpaper; it is the shape every reader already knows, a mark you look FOR rather than an accusation you learn to ignore; and IT FAILS SAFE - a relay whose key will not import vouches for nobody and so badges nobody, where the old polarity would have accused every head in the room at once. BESIDE THE NAME, NEVER INSIDE IT - the label is centred on the skull and a prefixed glyph walks it off the head, and only for the badged peers, so it reads as THOSE names drifting; its own draw in the classic face, its own `<span>` in the DOM one (which also means `setText` on the name cannot wipe it every frame). AND THE COLOUR STAYS WHERE IT WAS: SOC4's party green says WHO somebody is to you and the badge says whether the service issued the name - two systems, one pixel, and the colour belongs to the first; moving it up to the wrapper is what the first cut did and it broke two NAME1 pins that were right. ABSENT IS UNVOUCHED AND IT IS THE RELAY'S OWN ENCODING THAT SAYS SO: the wire has NO `v: false` - server/src/index.js sends `v: who.verified || undefined` and omits the field otherwise, a byte saved on the frame every player sends - so `e.peer.v === true` is the only reading that agrees with the sender. ONE ASCII GLYPH, AND ASCII IS NECESSARY RATHER THAN SUFFICIENT: `drawText` draws NOTHING AT ALL for a glyph of zero width, so a badge the Daggerfall font lacks is one the classic skin silently never shows while the DOM face shows it - a tick is the obvious badge and is not in that font, so NAME_MARK is `*`, held inside the font's own glyph range, and the limit is stated rather than left to be found (this container has no ARENA2, so the real FONT0003 is not read here). The fixture sets `v` EXPLICITLY and its default is NOT VOUCHED - the plain label, which is what those pins are about and what an ordinary peer is today. Two neighbouring pins were re-aimed rather than worked around and both got stronger: SOC4 read the white fallback off the draw call and reads it off the `tint` BOTH draws take (SOC4 owns that colour and this slice does not get a second opinion on it), and PERF-ON measured one draw per peer and measures both cases now - a badged room is 2n draws and still nothing that scales with how long anybody's name is. Mutants: acc1dmark.json (the file is gone with the slice - ACC1g retired the mark hours later), 8, 8 dead, TWO of which survived a first run: MARK-3 (prefixing the badge INTO the name leaves the RUN COUNT unchanged, so the pin reads the badged peer's name draw as having the same glyph count as when nobody is badged) and MARK-7 (the read flipped to `!== false` - every pin said `v: false` OUT LOUD, which is a frame the relay never sends; under this polarity it is the worse bug of the two, because the badge is the thing a player is asked to TRUST, so a mutant that badges the unchecked is a lie rather than a missing warning). MARK-8 holds Mac's correction as a LAW rather than as a memory: the polarity flipped back is a dead mutant, not a preference somebody can quietly restore. ACC2c GAVE THE BACKUP A WAY BACK (Mac, asked whether to build it: "And yes"). ACC2 BUILT THE BACKUP AND NOTHING COULD READ ONE BACK: `pullSlot` was written, pinned end to end against the real service in a real workerd, and had ZERO CALLERS - not a missing button but a missing SURFACE, because a cloud card only ever reached a player as the cloud LINE on a local tile and a card with no local tile has no line to appear on. So the one case cloud saves exist for did not work: a cleared browser or a second machine opened Load and read "No saved games. Save a game and every slot of it appears here" with the player's games three feet away in R2 - and that sentence is now one of the things this slice DELETES. THE DIFFERENCE IS A SET DIFFERENCE AND IT LIVES IN systems/: `cloudOnly(cards, saves)` keyed by slotKeyOf, the SAME key the cloud line is asked with, so a card gets a line on a tile or a tile of its own, NEVER BOTH AND NEVER NEITHER, and the pin asserts that card by card. It is not in the menu because that is AUDIT-312 F3 taken seriously rather than repeated - the last slot key written there had dropped the character half and the mutant of it went through the whole suite untouched; here the same mutant dies, and it matters because one local QuickSave would otherwise hide EVERY other character's cloud QuickSave. THE CARD IS SMALLER THAN A SAVE SO THE TILE SAYS LESS: server-account/src/saves.js keeps eleven columns and none is a race, a class, a level, a health, a gold or a look, so `saveFromCard` leaves those ABSENT - not zero, not a dash - and the tile degrades on its own (tileLine joins nothing and is never appended, the stats list stays empty and is never appended, the well falls back to the character's initial). NOT ONE SPECIAL CASE IN THE DRAWING AND NOT ONE INVENTED FACT, held by two mutants: a level read off `save_version`, and the initial taken from the slot name so a tile reads `QuickSave` where the character goes. `gameTime` IS CLASSIC MINUTES (saveSlots.js's own SaveInfo typedef, of the very field pushSlot copies up), so the date is derived by the same two calls a local tile's is. `only` IS A SEVENTH STATE AND ITS OWN WHOLE LADDER, because every rung of the local one is a question about a LOCAL slot - whether it predates CHARID1, whether its upload finished, whether it has been backed up at all - and none is answerable about a save that is not here; a card with no characterId is still a card, not a `wait`. What IS still true of it is kept: a download in flight is busy, a refused one carries the service's own word. The sentence is "Only in your backup" and not "Backed up", which under a tile whose only copy IS the backup tells a player they have two of something they have one of; it wears `is-only` and the probe reads it BRASS against the backed-up line's verdigris in a real Chromium. ONE PANE - LOAD, and no other: Online brings a character in to play NOW and cannot use a save that is not here, so offering it there is a two-step act at a one-step door, and Save writes rather than reads, so a cloud-only slot in that grid would be an Overwrite target for a game this device does not have. AND THE TILE CARRIES DELETE BACKUP, two presses, which is THE REST OF AUDIT-312 F1: that slice gave a player the way to act on "delete a save there to make room" and gave it to them on LOCAL tiles only, so a cloud-only slot went on holding its share of SAVES_MAX with no surface that could ever release it. Probe: npm run savetile 18 -> 29 checks, which is where the heading's face, the brass and a tile that could have collapsed to a strip were actually measured. Mutants: tools/mutants/acc2c.json, 10, 10 dead.
- **DAEDRA1** 2026-09-21, Dracula/Valentin on Discord ("daedra summoning is fucked"), with record 481 on screen reading "Today is %dat, the day of summoning for %dae. Do you, %pcn, wish to risk you life and very soul by summoning %dae into our mundane world?": THE COVEN HANDED TEXT.RSC OVER RAW. Its `rows` provider was a bare `townTalk.lines(id)`, where DFU's DaggerfallMessageBox runs MacroHelper over every record it is handed - so the coven's own prompts, its quest offers and the whole summoning flow it dispatches printed their symbols verbatim. The guild path beside it had had an expander since MACROS1; this one never got one. Two sources, and both are now wired: %dat and %pcn are MacroHelper's GLOBAL rows and resolve off the quest machine's context (ROAD-E E7's ONE GAMEMANAGER - the port's stand-in for GameManager.Instance, whichever window is expanding), while %dae is the FLOW's own, because which prince answers is decided by the day and the coven's roll and by nothing else. `summonMacroValues` is that one source; a null daedra leaves the token verbatim rather than printing a hole, which is MacroHelper's own null posture. `expandRowValues` grew its third argument to make it reachable: the walk took a value map and nothing else, so a caller with no values of its own could not ask for the shared table at all - MACROS1's "the table was filled for the symbols somebody expected" lesson, one level up, at the walk instead of the table. The greeting and the prince's own offer are expanded by the prince WHO ANSWERED rather than the one the day named (Sheogorath hijacks 5% of summonings), and each box reads its record ONCE - several carry random variants, so the `say(id).length ? say(id)` shape this slice first wrote would have rolled twice and could have shown the second roll. Caught before it shipped, and the mutant that proves it stays. 2 pins, 7 mutants, 7 dead.
- `10-UI/Enhanced-Maps-Arc.md` - **EM-BUG3** 2026-09-21, Mac from play ("enhanced local town maps are rotated wrong. Not exactly sure the correct location but i was in the corner of town and It thinks entirely different buildings are there"): THE PLAN AND THE NAMES STOOD IN TWO SPACES. `townBytes` copied `autoMapData` row for row, and an FLD grid's rows run AGAINST +Z - the port already reads that law everywhere else it touches one, as `groundTiles[x][15 - y]` in `buildGroundTilemap` (world/rmbLayout.js:270), and ExteriorAutomap.cs:1481 is the same law at 64 rows rather than 16. So every block's bytes lay MIRRORED north-south against the nameplate anchors, the quest rings and the player's own caret, all three of which come straight off +Z. Inside one block a tavern swapped ends with whatever faced it, which is exactly "different buildings are there" when you stand at the edge of town and look. The second half is the sheet's own orientation: compose the shipped window's two flips and they are ONE law, `(gridH-1-b.y)*64 + y_src` == `H-1-anchorRow` - the anchor formula seen from the screen, where a higher +Z is a higher row on the paper. EM4's note had read the shipped window as DISAGREEING with the anchor and drew the anchor row downward instead, which turned the enhanced plan over against the classic map of the same town. Both halves are one fix: the field is laid in the shipped window's screen space, and `sheetY` is the single transform everything arriving in anchor space crosses by - the plates, the quest rings and the caret, the last through `playerOnSheet` so the overlay and the rest view cannot cross on different paths. THE PIN THAT LET IT THROUGH is the one EM4 wrote for exactly this: it asserted the arithmetic as written ("local y 5 is field row 5") and so agreed with the bug, and its own note said the picture's rightness "is a browser probe's question and Mac's eyes'". Mac's eyes answered. It is re-aimed onto an equality between the two modules over four grid cells and four blocks, and the new pin stands a building and the player on ONE spot in the world and checks they come out on ONE spot on the paper - then checks the whole sheet against `buildExteriorLayout`, so the two maps of one town can never be mirrors again. 7 mutants, 7 dead.
- THREE FROM DISCORD (2026-09-21, Mac: "3 bugs"), each to its root cause and pinned: **MAC-D1** (SquidKamer, on the desktop app: "I cant seem to swing the weapon in the installed version... I have to enable the attack click but I prefer the mouse swing") - MAC-SWING1 had fixed the READ, so a swing bound to a key answers whatever it is bound to; it did not fix the STATE, an action with NO code at all. `setBinding` clears an action's old row when a new code takes it and `removedPrimary` is DFU's "keep this unbound" mark, which is exactly what the autofill pass obeys - so the swing can end up addressing nothing, and the desktop app's prefs file carries that across reinstalls, which is why it reads as an installed-build bug. `UNLOSEABLE_ACTIONS` are repaired at the one door every load comes through, and a PAD row does not count as reachable (the pad defaults refill every load, so a stranded swing still answered a joystick code while nothing on the desk could swing). **MAC-D2** (Skibbster, with a screenshot of a TOMATO on the "Small Cart" card) - transportation has no inventory art and the columns that look like it are ANOTHER template's: Small Cart and all four boats name player texture 213/1, which is the WORLD sprite of template 91, the Wine Rack. DFU's rows are not wrong there, because classic never draws a vehicle in a list - transportation is a flag you buy, not a thing you carry - so those columns were never read; the port keeps a cart as a real item (that is how the wagon knows it exists) and so read columns nobody maintained. No address now rather than a wrong one. **MAC-D3** (Seanobi: "stuck in an infinite deathloop. Instant death after respawning") - the online respawn healed on its LAST line, after `await _teleportToPixel` and after forceExitToExterior had torn the death screen down. The frame loop only holds off raising death while a DeathScreen is up, so every frame of that await saw a dead player and no screen: death, reset, respawn, death, and a teleport loads a map pixel, so under the lag spike he reported the window is many frames wide and the loop cannot be escaped from inside the game. The heal is synchronous and first now, before anything is torn down or awaited - the order the dungeon arm of the same law already had - and one respawn is in flight at a time. `respawnHealth` also answers a living number for any input: it used to return NaN for a corrupt maxHealth, and a NaN health is neither alive nor dead. 3 pins, 1 re-aimed, 8 mutants dead.
- `10-UI/Enhanced-Maps-Arc.md` - EM-BUG1/EM-BUG2 2026-09-21, Mac from play ("your equipped weapon isnt stowed when the map is out"; "You cannot press the M key to stow the map"): TWO THE TABS COST, BOTH FROM THE ARC'S OWN CENTRAL MOVE - three windows became one, and two laws written for the travel map alone did not widen with it. **EM-BUG1:** MAP-WEAPON's gate (`if (sheetWindowUp()) return;`) rode on `isTravelMap`, and EM4 correctly narrowed that tag to "the bay is reachable from here" for the TRAVEL question - one flag answering two questions, and narrowing it for one silently un-answered the other, so a town or crypt sheet stopped hiding the weapon. `holdsScreen` is its own constant tag now and `isTravelMap` answers only what it is named for. AND three hosts never had the gate at all: it was wired in world.js alone, because when MAP-WEAPON was written no other host could open the sheet - so the dungeon automap would have kept the sword even with the flag fixed. All four read their own slot. **EM-BUG2:** the close arm took the TravelMap action alone, and every door EM3/EM4 added is behind the AUTOMAP key, so the key that opened the map could not shut it; the classic twin has always taken its own binding back, and this one takes both now. WHAT LET THE FIRST THROUGH: the pin asserted the WIRING, so when EM4 changed the wiring the pin was updated to match and went green over the regression. It asserts the law now. 1 pin re-aimed, 1 new, 9 mutants dead.
- `05-Combat/Blood-Arc.md` - AUDIT BLOOD3 F9/F10 2026-09-21, Mac ("Whats the meniscus", then "Might aswell" - make the picture): TRYING TO PHOTOGRAPH THE MENISCUS FOUND TWO MORE. **F9:** setting `BLOOD_MENISCUS` to `0.0` to shoot it against itself STOPPED THE SHADER COMPILING - JavaScript stringifies `0.0` as `"0"`, GLSL reads an INTEGER literal, and `vec3 * int` has no overload. Every dial this arc interpolated was bare, so any of them at a round value was a runtime landmine under exactly the knobs the record invites a reader to tune; `glslFloat` (airPass.js:211) already existed and the lantern loop had used it since EL5. Eleven interpolations routed through it in both lanes, pinned by BUILDING the shader at an integral value rather than by reading the built string. **F10:** the picture then said the record was wrong about what the term does. Peak change 42/255, so it is plainly working - but twice as strong in the BODY as in the rim band (22.8 against 2.1), because a pool's thickness is a smoothstep and a smoothstep is steepest in the MIDDLE of its ramp. It lights the mark as a relief of its own thickness, which is right and is what gives a pool volume; "a pool has a raised edge, so its rim catches light" is not what it computes. A lens flagged that wording during the audit and it was carried into the record anyway. The meniscus now has a probe row (35/35) - the one law this arc took to main with no picture at all. 2 pins, 3 re-aimed, 33 mutants dead.
- `05-Combat/Blood-Arc.md` - AUDIT BLOOD3 2026-09-21, Mac ("audit this real quick. Make sure it's perfect"): IT WAS NOT. THREE LENSES, AND TWO FOUND THE SAME HEADLINE DEFECT INDEPENDENTLY. **F1: the film ran BACKWARDS.** `shapeAt`'s own docstring said the sheet holds "a darkening toward the heart of a pool" and every shape obeyed it - minimum ink at the THICKEST point - and BLOOD3 read high ink as thick. Same quantity, sign reversed: the film brightened hardest exactly where the art had darkened, and the ink was still an albedo factor besides, so one grey channel was spent twice in opposite directions. Fixed at the source: `shapeAt` answers a DEPTH, the painter writes `1 - INK_DEPTH * (depth * grain)` on one shared ramp, the ink keeps its old range and sense (so the 2D blood lens that shares the sheet is untouched), and both shaders recover the thickness exactly and no longer multiply by the ink at all. **F2:** the meniscus differenced the neighbours' bare alpha against the centre's alpha*ink - a constant pedestal over every mark's body, a fixed diagonal tilt several times the rim signal, feeding the diffuse, the shadow offset, the ambient and the Fresnel; and `dFdx` sat inside a branch (undefined per GLSL ES 3.0) while the same values existed two lines up. **F3:** the classic set applied the film in display space and the lane in linear, so a thin rim came out up to 1.7x brighter there - W6's fault with the sign reversed. **F4/F5:** Schlick for a specular lobe is F(V.H), not F(N.V); BLOOD3's hoisted F(N.V) peaked in a different regime from the ^64 lobe it scaled and took a mark underfoot from 0.9 of the lamp to 0.018 - "less shiny" had become "not wet". The angle sits beside each light's own half-vector now, and a WET_DARKEN carries the cue you can actually see from above. Plus F6 (the depth gate never bit), F7 (a thinning film desaturates, it does not "warm"), F8 (a toFixed that its own pin reproduced). AND THE VERIFICATION WAS WRONG TOO: the probe's film check banded by red, so it was measuring the alpha-faded rim and would have passed with the film deleted; the parity fixtures had been moved to the one value where the film is a no-op; the sun's wet row had been relaxed to where a deleted glint passes. All three fixed, and a new pin walks the real sheet per kind - the pin that would have caught F1. 4 pins, 5 re-aimed, 28 mutants dead, 3 records re-aimed.
- `05-Combat/Blood-Arc.md` - BLOOD3 2026-09-21, Mac ("I think the blood is too shiny and flat"): THE FILM AND THE SHEEN. Flat, because the decal's albedo was a WHITE ink times ONE tint, so every texel of a pool came out the same red - a sticker. Blood is a film, and a film's colour is its depth: Beer-Lambert over `BLOOD_ABSORB` [0.50, 0.95, 0.95], anchored so full thickness is exactly a no-op (the AUDIT 4 lighting-parity law survives untouched), and the thickness is coverage times the INK'S GRAIN, not alpha alone - alpha is also the blend factor, so the rim the film brightens is the rim fading out and the body has no variation left. Shiny, because `EL_WET_STRENGTH` was 0.9 of the lamp at ANY angle - polished plastic. Schlick with water's F0 = 0.02: two per cent head-on (a mark underfoot, the case seen most), most of the light only at a graze, 0.55 for what is left, gated on the mark's own depth so a dried rim does not shine. Plus the meniscus - the thickness gradient through the quad's own tangent frame, tilting the normal AWAY from the rise, before `sunVis` so the shadows read it. Classic set takes the film; the lane takes all three. 3 pins, 2 re-aimed, 14 mutants dead, probe 31/31 on a real GPU.
- `10-UI/Enhanced-Maps-Arc.md` - ACTIVE, opened 2026-09-21 (EM0, Mac: "Next thing we are going to work on is the enhanced automap/town map. The goal is using the existing enhanced map infrastructure and imprinting on that. And instead of 3 seperate keybinds, adding a tab toggle on the map itself. So if you open it in a dungeon, the world map would be not accessible, same for the town map. Additionally, we're going to put our own spin on the automap and town map themselves, enhancing the look like how we did we the world map. The automap should become a 2d map and floor based instead of the current 3D implementation, streamlining it"). ONE MAP, THREE SHEETS. `Held-Map-Arc.md` built the enhanced WORLD map on Mac's own sprite and left the other two as DFU's own windows, ported whole (`ROAD-C c2/S5`-`S10`, `AUDIT 67`) - a real 3D pass in a 320x200 panel with a cut plane for the dungeon, a CPU composition of rotated quads for the town. Faithful, and unreadable next to the world map. They become TABS on the one held sheet: the paper, the hands, the thumbs, the pan, the zoom and the Morrowind held pose are the WINDOW, and what is inked is the SHEET. Which tabs a place offers is DERIVED off the flags every host already keeps rather than declared by a host - `systems/mapTabs.js`, one module because the question is asked in four hosts (dungeon automap, building automap, town, wilderness) and a fourth host that forgets is how a player reads a town map from inside a crypt. EM1 SHIPPED 2026-09-21 (the tab law: dungeon and building offer the automap alone, a town offers town+world, the wilderness the world alone; 5 pins). EM2 SHIPPED the same day (`systems/automapFloors.js` - Daggerfall HAS NO FLOOR FIELD: the `DungeonBlock` record parses x and z and no y, every block is laid at y zero, `originY` appears nowhere in `src/`, so the storeys are DERIVED off the triangles the reveal index already carries. **THE ARC'S FIRST REAL FINDING, from a fixture rather than from thought: a ramp welded two storeys into one.** The first cut had ONE threshold - the motor's own walk limit - and clustered the walkable heights by gaps; two rooms with a ramp between them came back as ONE storey, and it was right to, because a ramp puts a walkable triangle at EVERY height between the floors it joins, so a gap-walk has no gap to find. Every real dungeon has ramps, so that cut would have answered "one floor" for most of the game. THE CODE WAS FIXED, NOT THE TEST: two thresholds, and the split is the whole idea - `LEVEL_NY` (twenty degrees off flat) picks the triangles allowed to VOTE for a storey, `FLOOR_NY` (`SLOPE_LIMIT_DEG`, the motor's own, one home) picks the triangles DRAWN on it. A ramp is walked, drawn, and votes for nothing. A storey's height is the AREA-WEIGHTED mean of its voters, so one great hall decides a floor's level and a stray step does not move it, and the plan is a COASTLINE - the walkable cells rasterised and outlined by `boundarySegments` + `linkSegments`, inkMap's own two functions, so the dungeon and the Iliac Bay are drawn by the same hand; 8 pins over hand-built geometry). RECORDED EXCEPTION, because Mac's answer could not be taken literally: he chose "TravelMap (V) retired from the registry", and it cannot leave - `TravelMap` is one of DFU's OWN action rows (`inputActions.js` mirrors SetupDefaults, `ACTIONS.length` is pinned) and the CLASSIC skin still opens DFU's own travel map on it. The row stays and what it OPENS changes: on the enhanced skin V is a second door into the one window, landing on the world tab where the place offers one. EM1 SHIPPED the same day (`ui/mapStrip.js` - the tab toggle INKED on the parchment in PAPER coordinates rather than laid over it as DOM, because paper coordinates are the only space that survives MAP3's hands lane: a DOM tab would float in front of the Morrowind arm, while inked tabs go round the corner with the sheet and are reached through the same inverse the marks are picked through. Plus the SHEET CONTRACT the window is now routed through - the SPACE, the INK and the POINTER, with keys and chrome deliberately left out until EM3 gives them a second implementation to be derived from, because a hook shaped from one implementation comes out the wrong shape. The window keeps the paper, the hands, the pan, the zoom and the held pose and NEVER asks which sheet is up - a pin walks the class and fails on any comparison against a sheet's name. THE MID-FLIGHT GATE: the slot's offer is the place's offer narrowed by the sheets the window actually holds, so EM1 shipping the world sheet alone means the strip reads "The Bay" everywhere and nothing a player can reach has moved; the narrowing is over the window's own sheet map rather than a literal, so it lifts itself when EM3/EM4 land and until then a tab that inks nothing cannot be shipped. The KEY WIRING waits with them: a key that opens a blank sheet is a regression. 11 + 7 pins, 31 mutants dead). THE EM2 CAMPAIGN PAID FOR ITSELF: seven of twenty-nine mutants survived the first run, every one because a fixture was too tidy - quads fed bottom to top proved no sort, one winding proved the inside test never agreed with the facing test, every room having a flat floor proved no cave of pure slope, and FLOOR_MIN_GAP held only BY VALUE let a hardcoded 3.0 through (the motor's capsule is 1.8 and the headroom 1.2, so it agrees today and stops agreeing the day the player's height moves - held at the SOURCE now). The last survivor taught the module about itself: the `len > 0` facing guard looks redundant beside the area guard, since a zero-area triangle is dropped either way - but a NaN vertex gives area NaN, and `NaN < MIN_TRI_AREA` is FALSE, so a bad position sails past the area test and lands at y NaN where it drags a storey to nowhere. 6 more pins, 27 dead 2 equivalent. EM3/EM4 (the two sheets' ink, and the key) and EM5 (records, probes) open.
- `06-Systems/Systems-Arc.md` - CHARID1 2026-09-21, a player on Discord through Mac ("he created a new character and it overwrote his save"): A CHARACTER IS AN ID, NOT A NAME. A save's identity was (characterName, saveName), SaveLoadManager's own, so a new character who took an old one's name wrote its first QuickSave over the old character's. Every character carries an id now - minted at chargen and at a classic-save import, an envelope field, on the slot card - and a save's identity is (characterId, saveName); a card without an id never matches an id, a legacy card is adopted by its own character at load, both hosts and both save windows find and overwrite by id. The lost save is not recoverable - the write was the old slot's. 5 pins, 15 mutants dead. **The lesson: identity that can be typed is not identity.**
- `01-Overview/Desktop-App.md` - SP1 2026-09-21, a player on Discord ("my saves its all gone") and Mac ("we need parity between browser and the install"): SAVES MOVE BETWEEN THE WEBSITE AND THE APP. Nothing was deleted - the site keeps a save in the browser's storage and the app keeps it as files, two stores with one shape and nothing carrying between them. `systems/saveTransfer.js` exports every slot as one STORED zip in the app's own on-disk layout (a backup a player can open) and imports that zip or a picked Saves folder into whatever store is under the build, never overwriting a slot and never doubling a save already held; the doors are a Move-saves card on the enhanced Load pane, title and pause. Pinned by a round trip through the port's own zip reader; 12 mutants dead. **Earlier on the same page:** REL2 2026-09-21, a player ("install to a specific directory ... installing to Temp/.../Daggerfall Enhanced.exe"): EVERY WINDOWS RELEASE CARRIED ONE EXE, THE PORTABLE. The two Windows targets shared one artifact name and the second overwrote the first; the surviving portable unpacks into %TEMP% and has no directory to choose. Distinct names now (-setup, -portable), the NSIS installer assisted with the directory page. REL3 the same day (Mac: "auto push a release on each merge?"): every main push cuts a release at a version DERIVED from the commit (base MAJOR.MINOR, patch the commit count) into one workflow variable the tag and the stamp both read; the marker file and REL1's gate retired. DA7 the same day (Mac: "Is there an easy way to make it where players dont have to manually install each release?" / "Do it"): THE DESKTOP APP UPDATES IN PLACE - electron-updater on the NSIS install and the AppImage, downloads on its own, installs on quit, behind DA6's two gates; macOS, the portable exe and an unpackaged run keep the notice (one pure table). Three files pinned because none imports another: the shell's settings, the build's publish PROVIDER (without it no latest.yml is ever written, whatever --publish says) and the workflow attaching the metadata. 4 pins, 9 mutants dead; the first release after the merge is the first that carries it.
- `07-Rendering/Performance-Rig.md` - GROUND-LAST 2026-09-21, Mac ("I want proper fucking fixes"): THE GROUND IS DRAWN AFTER THE MESHES. It was the first draw of every pixel, so every ground fragment under every building and tree was shaded in full and painted over; drawn after the opaque meshes it fails the depth test before its shader runs. The streaming world queues each pixel's ground and drains the queue after the walk (a pixel's ground under the next pixel's buildings too; the terrain program bound once a frame); the town host draws its ground after the buildings, the mills, the rig and the arrows. Sky, ring, water and flats keep their places after it. Not measured on a GPU; the saving is the ground under a mesh, on a GPU-bound frame.
- `06-Systems/Online-Arc.md` - DROPS 2026-09-22, Mac ("Alright this is a big one. These changes are made specific for our codebase"): FOUR DROPS INTEGRATED - QUEST1 (a quest shared with the party through the catalog's own gates, a fourth door a live quest is born through), TRADE1 (player-to-player trade: the state machine under LOOT-DUP's law, 5 m between the bodies, the F-menu's third row, the enhanced window, the relay routing one peer's frame), PARTY-REST1/2/3 (the leader's nap mirrored by members near them, `/ready`), PEER-FS1/2 (peers' footsteps and swings off the pose's `fk`, two local switches), SEAT-HEAL (a joiner that took the dungeon's seat gives it back) - block-level merges against each drop's true base, the brand mark and the class-omit law restored, a `ui/restDoor.js` that does not exist un-imported, the relay ONE bump to world91 (the merge deploys it - `relay-deploy.yml` drift-deploys off `RELAY_VERSION`). - `07-Rendering/Performance-Rig.md` - PEER-CADENCE 2026-09-22, Mac ("look for ways to improve online performance"): A PEER'S BODY IS RE-POSED ON A CADENCE THE EYE CAN SEE. PERF-ON2 never measured the peer bodies, and they are the online frame's one real cost: every standing peer's rig was skinned in JS, re-uploaded and particle-stepped every frame against a pose that arrives ten times a second and a sprite drawn in 3-px blocks. The rig's update takes `pose: false` now (the clocks advance, the skin waits) and PeerBodies poses by distance - every frame within 10 m, every second to 25 m, every third beyond, staggered by body, the first frame of a standing body always posed, the skipped dt banked for the particles. Measured on the real rig: 1.00 poses a body a frame before, 0.74 / 0.52 / 0.43 after at 1 / 4 / 8 bodies walking out. 7 pins, 15 mutants dead. Open: the sprite render is still one a body a frame (one shared target); the foes' rigs could take the same cadence. AUDIT PEER-CADENCE (same day, three lenses): the stagger was an accident of when builds landed (a per-body tick; the module counts frames now), a body back from far or a linger drew a skin seconds old, and a rebuild that let the mesh go stood the doll for a frame - all three paid and pinned by execution; 11 pins, 20 mutants dead.
- `07-Rendering/Performance-Rig.md` - PERF-READ1 2026-09-21, Mac's `?perf=cpu` readout (`hud 7.09 | ... | world 0.06`, `hud 10.78`): THE `hud` SPAN WAS THE VSYNC WAIT. A CPU span closes at the next mark, the next mark after the frame's last was the NEXT frame's first, and the enhanced skin's HUD is DOM with no screen quad - so `hud` ran through the rAF wait and the totals summed to the frame period; the same deferral printed `draws 0` on every enhanced-skin line. `stopCpu()` at the script frame's end, the owed resolve before the new frame's reset, a `ui` span after the HUD's draw. Pinned on a fake clock (hud 1, not 10). **The lesson: the last span of a frame has no next mark of its own - close it by hand or it measures the wait.**
