- `07-Rendering/Performance-Rig.md` - PERF-RIG1 + PERF-ZONE2 2026-09-21, Mac ("continue looking into fixing exterior performance issues"): THE `world` ZONE OPENED. It was three spans of the host's frame, and in them the Morrowind rig's whole geometry pipeline on the CPU once per body per frame - the arm, the third-person body, every peer's: `skinBatch` minting ~156 KB of typed-array garbage per 3,000-vertex piece per call (off-heap, invisible to the heap meter) and `packFpArm` three arrays per corner. The accumulators are the batch's, the pack keeps each piece's static lanes, the ranges keep their identity; every float bit-for-bit, pinned against the old code as the reference. Five new CPU zones name what `world` swallowed. Still on the CPU: GPU skinning is the recorded next step.
- `05-Combat/Dwarven-Thunderlock.md` - FIELD-GUN19 2026-09-20, Mac ("The muzzle flash texture itself needs to be blue like the orb and the lighting thats emitted needs to be blue" / "the orb projectile that shoots doesnt line up with the muzzle. It shoots out high on the screen"): TWO REPORTS, ONE SPECIES - a number that answered a question nobody had asked, and both had already been "fixed" by FIELD-GUN17. **THE SHOT CAME OUT HIGH** because the muzzle is a CANVAS PIXEL and the orb is a WORLD OBJECT, and ROAD-E E5 made those different spaces: the docked large HUD SHRINKS the world pass while the 2D pass takes the whole canvas back, so dividing the pixel by the taller of the two gives a larger `up` - 96% too high on a fifth-of-the-screen bar. The line's own comment said "the viewmodel is drawn over the whole frame", which is TRUE OF THE GUN and says nothing about where the orb lands; E5 had already converted the crosshair and the tap pick for this exact reason and the viewmodel's muzzle was the third consumer nobody counted. `worldRectPx` (tapRay's own arithmetic, the part with the flip in it) is reused; its BOUNDS TEST is deliberately not, because a tap outside the strip is not a pick but a muzzle below it is a real point below the bottom of the view. The renderer keeps the rect as a RECORD past `endWorldPass`, where the pixel rect is GL STATE and is rightly cleared there. **THE FLASH STAYED WHITE** although the sampling channel was wired correctly: the fault was the STATISTIC. A glowing missile sprite is a blown-out white core inside a coloured halo, and the core is the brightest and most opaque thing in the record, so an alpha-weighted MEAN was dominated by it and peak-normalising made it paler still - a lavender indistinguishable from the white it replaced, which is why a channel that worked looked like it had never been wired. A texel with no saturation carries no information about what colour a thing is, so the weight is alpha TIMES CHROMA and only the halo votes (the old mean gave 125,152,255 where this gives 68,107,255); the greyscale fallback is part of the rule, not a guard, because a white orb should answer white. THREE CAMPAIGN SURVIVORS, each a fixture blind to its own hole: the HUD bar is at the BOTTOM so the strip's top edge is canvas row 0 and dropping that term changed nothing; a getter over a field nobody assigns answers null for ever and the pin only asked whether the getter EXISTED; and chroma WITHOUT alpha passed because everything chromatic in the fixture was blue. 10 mutants, 10 killed. **The lesson: "we already fixed that" is a claim about a commit, not about a pixel - both of these had a correct mechanism fed by the wrong number.**
- `05-Combat/Dwarven-Thunderlock.md` - FIELD-GUN-MW3 2026-09-20, Mac ("And this is rigged properly to the morrowind model?"): **NO - IT WAS ATTACHED, WHICH IS NOT THE SAME THING**, and the three gaps were measured rather than argued. THE GUN WAS 1.43 CM LONG: Morrowind is 69.99 units to the metre and MW2 normalised the mesh to a longest axis of 1, a tidy number for a MESH and a meaningless one for a WEAPON; it bakes at 0.75 m x 69.99 = 52.5 units now, a length taken from the weapon's own row (`isOneHanded: false`, `baseWeight` 6.0 - the heaviest in the game) rather than from taste. THE FIST CLOSED ROUND THE RECEIVER: the bone is a HAND and the mesh pivoted on its bounds centre, so the origin is the centroid of the rearmost band of the long axis now, derived from geometry. AND THE ARMS WERE PUNCHING: `resolveWeaponParts` returned `MW_WEAPON_TYPE.None` and `animWeaponType` turns None into HandToHand, so the rig played unarmed stances with a dwemer firearm along for the ride - the row names a type to BORROW (MarksmanCrossbow: two-handed, ranged, and the ONLY type `reloadsItself` is true for, which is the lab's own finding about this weapon's cycle) while explicitly not borrowing its AMMUNITION, because `ammoTypeFor` of a crossbow is Bolt and the arm would put a quarrel through the gun. A WEAPON TYPE IS NOT A MODEL: the model had to be ours because Morrowind has no gun, the animation did not, because Morrowind has something shaped exactly like this act. Re-baking at the right size then put a BLACK RECTANGLE on the receiver, which was an unwrap bug and a bad one: MW2's island walk compared each face to the NEIGHBOUR it joined across, and fold tolerance CHAINS - on an eight-sided barrel every adjacent pair is 45 degrees apart, inside 66, so the walk went round the whole ring, whose average normal is nearly zero, and 78 of 295 triangles came out with real 3D area and ZERO UV area (the largest fifty square units), each painting its whole face with one texel. Islands grow against their OWN RUNNING AVERAGE now, which is sound by construction. **AND THE LAST PIN IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST**: a campaign left SIX survivors with one cause between them - the committed .nif and .dds are the output of a run that already happened, so mutating the tools that made them changed nothing any pin could see. Mac's .fbx is committed now and the suite RE-RUNS the whole chain over it and compares byte for byte, twice; all six died the moment that existed. 60 mutants over MW2 and MW3, 60 killed. **The lesson: "it binds to the right bone" is a fact about a data structure and says nothing about size, pivot or motion - and a gate that reads a committed artefact is testing the artefact, not the thing that made it.**
- `05-Combat/Dwarven-Thunderlock.md` - FIELD-GUN-MW2 2026-09-20, Mac ("Continue. Figure this out yourself."): **THE GUN IS IN THE HAND, AND THE FBX HAD NO UNWRAP AT ALL.** MW1 left a bake nothing drew; this draws it, and the blocker found on the way is a measurement rather than an opinion: the export's `LayerElementUV` is Blender's FACTORY CYLINDER MAPPING - 45 distinct coordinates on exact eighths, the triangles' UV areas totalling 8.49 (1.0 fills an atlas once), EVERY covered texel claimed by more than one triangle and one by THIRTY-NINE - so any texture at all puts the same pixels on the barrel, the receiver and the grip. The first bake proved it by returning four deliberately different bands within three levels of each other. So the unwrap is COMPUTED (islands by a 66-degree fold over WELDED positions, each projected on its area-weighted normal, squared to its minimum-area rectangle, shelf-packed at ONE global scale so a texel is the same size in world units everywhere), and the texture is BAKED OFF THE GEOMETRY rather than painted blind - the mesh rasterised into its own UV space, so every texel knows its position, its normal and how enclosed it is, with the occlusion CAST against the real triangles because it is the one shading term that belongs in a texture at all. THE MESH BECOMES A NIF, because `bindPartsInto` does `parseNif(part.bytes)` and a part IS NIF BYTES: teaching the bind a second entrance would have to re-implement or skip rule 34's discarded root transform, rule 14's BoneOffset, rule 13's mirror, the property chain and the attach, for ever, for one mesh - and this file's own history is that "MW7 failed by carrying a second port of one rule". `tools/nifWrite.mjs` is held to the strictest check that exists: the pin parses what it wrote with THE PORT'S OWN `parseNif` and flattens it with THE PORT'S OWN `flattenNif`, and a 4.0.0.2 stream has no record sizes, so a writer four bytes out cannot produce a file that parses at all. It reaches the arm through three seams and no new machinery: a TABLE of the weapons Morrowind does not have (not a branch - the Thunderlock is "THE FIRST ONE OF ITS KIND" and the second must not be a second `if`), an archive mounted AFTER the player's loose files so Mac's own texture overrides ours without a rebuild, and `makeVendoredArchive` moved out of one vendored mod's module now that it has a second caller (twice is a law). FOUR BUGS THE PINS CAUGHT THAT AN EYE WOULD HAVE CALLED ART: the packer's scale bisection was CAPPED at its starting bound (returning exactly 1 for a mesh whose longest axis is 1, leaving sixty per cent of the atlas empty); the texture's bands were measured from the axis MINIMUM, which is the butt, so the sooted-muzzle band painted the grip and only the two middle bands looked wrong; the palette was typed in screen values when the shader wants linear; and a verdigris keyed on occlusion was REMOVED rather than tuned, because this mesh's mean AO is 0.60 and the recessed plates along the barrel are exactly the places that are both occluded and the most visible surface on the weapon. TWO GATES WERE BLIND AND BOTH ARE PAID: `itemMapCoverage`'s population was `WEAPONS`, so the one weapon in the port with no Morrowind model was the one it never asked about (the count is DERIVED off the own-models table now, because being outside a hand-counted population was the defect); and the allow-list's forward read covered `public/` alone while its own reverse read already covered `src/assets/`, so a new file bundled from there needed no row. 25 pins, 45 mutants (45 dead) after two survivors were re-aimed - a mesh with no normals, and a vertex shared by two islands, neither of which any fixture had. **The lesson: "it has UVs" is not "it has an unwrap", and the difference is one number nobody had computed - a texture over a factory mapping does not look untextured, it looks painted, just painted wrong.**
- `05-Combat/Dwarven-Thunderlock.md` - FIELD-GUN-MW1 2026-09-20, Mac (uploading `Pellet_Shot.fbx`: "Next up is texturing and rigging this for the morrowind model"): **IN MORROWIND MODE THE GUN IS NOT THERE, AND THE PORT ALREADY SAID SO.** `resolveWeaponParts` resolves the held weapon through `dfWeaponToMw`, which walks DFU's frozen `WEAPONS` table; the Thunderlock is template 560, registered at runtime, so the lookup answers `None`, no part is pushed, and the note it leaves is verbatim "weapon: Morrowind has no weapon type for what you are holding" - the arm draws EMPTY HANDS. That is the resolve being exactly right about a weapon Morrowind does not have: every mesh in that lane comes out of the PLAYER'S OWN INSTALL by search token, and no token finds a dwemer firearm in data that has none. `mwItemMap`'s "TOTAL, AND HONEST" census cannot see it either, because its population is `WEAPONS` - the one genuinely unmapped weapon in the port is the one its coverage walk never asks about. **So the model has to be ours**, which is why Mac sent an FBX - and the file, despite its name, is THE GUN: rendered, it is unmistakably a short pistol-grip firearm. `tools/fbxRead.mjs` + `tools/fbxMesh.mjs` bake a binary FBX into the shape `flattenNif` already emits, so nothing downstream needs a new consumer and no FBX parser ships to a player (the `tools/gunPaperdoll.mjs` precedent one dimension down: raw art in ignored `scratch/`, the port's own asset out). The bake triangulates (refusing a CONCAVE n-gon by name rather than fanning it into a shard), welds on the (position, normal, uv) TRIPLE, bakes the non-uniform scale as the geometry it is and gives its normals the INVERSE TRANSPOSE, and lands in a frame whose two axes are named and whose third is their cross product - the default measured off the model, not guessed. WHAT THE BAKE FOUND, verified: the FBX carries no `Material`, `Texture`, `Deformer` or `LimbNode` at all, so there is no texture and no skeleton - "rigging" here is the ATTACH, and `tools/meshSheets.mjs --uv` draws the unwrap as the sheet to paint on; and the mesh is OPEN, 65 of its edges shared by one face and 65 of its 199 positions on a boundary, though its winding is consistent throughout. The fixture is WRITTEN by the test - a forty-line binary-FBX writer - so no blob nobody can read in a diff enters the tree. 9 pins, 29 mutants (29 dead) after TWO were re-aimed for being vacuous: the handedness assertion recomputed `f x u` from `f` and `u`, which the mirror mutant never touched, and the weld fixture varied only one part of its key. Nothing draws the mesh yet, on purpose - the wiring needs a texture that does not exist and an orientation settled against a real weapon bone, and a mesh in `public/` that nothing loads is the dead seam this project keeps deleting. **The lesson: the census that exists to make coverage honest was blind to the one item it should have been loudest about, because its population was a list of the things somebody else had already thought of.**
- `01-Overview/Audit-68.md` - AUDIT 68, THE WHOLE-TREE SWEEP (PARTIAL) 2026-09-24, Mac ("a deep comprehensive audit across the entirety of the codebase... No band aids"): 53 finder lanes with adversarial verifiers, file-disjoint fix clusters in worktrees integrated in green checkpoints, a four-lane review of the whole diff before merge. 22 clusters landed (about 200 findings, each behavioural fix pinned in `test/audit68_*.test.js`), relay `world103`, desktop shell on Electron 42; cut short by the account's session limit - what was never audited or never fixed is listed on the page.
- `01-Overview/Audit-Thunderlock.md` - CLOSED 2026-09-19: THE GUN LAB, THE WEAPON, AND WHAT A GREEN SUITE WAS HIDING (Mac: "Lets fo a comprehensive audit over everything"), over the ten commits that built the Dwarven Thunderlock - the lab page (`05-Combat/Gun-Lab.md`), its Freesound/DAGGER.SND clips and art, the weapon itself (`05-Combat/Dwarven-Thunderlock.md`), its enchanted variant and unique find, and the Weapon Widget motion extraction underneath them. **Eight findings, all fixed**, and the first six are ONE SPECIES: the tests imported the module under test and the game imported nothing, so a registration-at-import module was green in the suite and ABSENT from the running game - **F1**, nothing in `src/` imported `systems/thunderlock.js`, so the weapon had no template row, could never drop and had no icons, and every finding below it was downstream of a weapon that did not exist. **F2** `machine.ranged` was written and read nowhere, so the gun paid no cooldown and no reload pause. **F3** the FOURTH HOST (`exterior.js`) was unconverted, so in the open world the gun fell to the melee arc and spent arrows. **F4** the Dwemer Pellet was rarity-eligible, and a promotion ENCHANTS it - an enchanted item does not stack, so a find of twenty becomes twenty rows. **F5** the out-of-ammo guard was bow-only: at zero pellets a trigger that does nothing and says nothing. **F6** `deploy.yml` checked out the FEATURE BRANCH BY NAME, so deleting it after the merge would have taken the whole Pages deploy down, the game's and not only the lab's. **F7** assets resolved against `document.baseURI` and the game's document is `/play/index.html` - the weapon would have drawn NOTHING, which is MAP-FIELD four weeks earlier on Mac's own held-map sprite, so the site-root law moved out to `systems/appRoot.js` (twice is a law). **F8** the clips were baked, allow-listed, documented and wired into the LAB, and `machineStep` emits `bowSound` only for a bow - the weapon fired in silence in all four hosts, and the lab having sound made that HARDER to notice. One finding was refused with its reason written down (the pellet's icon does NOT fall to the Arrow's art: `usesWorldTexture` is true for any custom template), because a wrong finding that goes unrecorded gets re-found. `hostCombat.js`'s weapon-to-skill table turned out to be a second copy answering `null` for the new weapon - the thing that sent the gun down the melee arc before F3 was even reachable - and is one table now. 7 new pins (`test/thunderlock.test.js` is 17), each pinning the question THE WAY THE GAME ASKS IT: F1 imports a host, F3/F5 read the four hosts' source, F7 drives the root resolver with the URLs both lanes actually produce.
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - DSH1 2026-09-20, Mac ("The far away horizon is still viewable even though it's cloudy"): A HARD RED LINE ALONG THE WHOLE HORIZON UNDER A FULL OVERCAST LID - and the sky in that frame is not the port's. The dome was swept first, in the sky lab with no game data, across seven weathers and nine hours: its below-horizon reading is never red and never more saturated than (172,144,135), because `skyState` greys `pal.horizon` by the weather's `grey` before anything reads it. The same sweep under `?sky=dynamic` answered in one run - (231,119,48), (211,100,48), (82,18,40), saturated warm colours that do not move when the weather does. **Dynamic Skies**, `Enabled: true` by default and the third tier of the Enhanced Environments row, so a player who never opened the mod's panel is running it. Two causes: the volumetric march closes every far bank on `uHorizonColor` and `cloudsStateUnderMod` handed it the mod's `RenderSettings.fogColor` RAW, so a colour the mod's own dome shows in a half-degree strip was being painted across the entire far ring - it takes the port's own weather greying now, toward the DECK'S shade rather than a fixed grey, and sunny is grey 0 and comes through 1:1; and the lid stopped short of the ground twice over (the composite discarded at `el <= 0.0`, the march's near early-out answered "clear sky"), so the mod's hot strip came through underneath it - both closed, the near early-out giving the same `vec4(uHorizonColor, 0.0)` the far one already gave and the composite carrying the bottom row over a SKIRT sized to Unity's own `SKY_GROUND_THRESHOLD`. A skirt and not a floor: an earlier cut held that row to the nadir and turned a clear dusk's ground half into a flat opaque slab, a new artefact traded for the old. Three earlier attempts changed nothing on screen and none was checked before the next was written; a green marker below the horizon settled it in one run by showing the red line surviving ABOVE the green. 5 pins, 18 mutants (18 dead), DS2's horizon pin and VC3's two composite pins re-aimed by content. **The lesson: the port chooses numbers on a vendored mod's behalf, and those choices are the PORT'S - "1:1" covers the mod's shader, not the state the port synthesises to feed its own passes.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - WEEDS1 2026-09-19, Mac ("its better, what else can we do?"): EVERY WEED IN THE WORLD WAS CASTING INTO THE 240-UNIT CASCADE. F5 has culled casters too small to shadow a texel since the standing-shadows report - and for FLATS it was dead, because it measures the BATCH'S SPHERE and a billboard batch is every flat of one (archive, record) across a whole streamed pixel, which is 128 tiles at 6.4 units: 819 across. A batch of ankle-high weeds therefore carries a sphere of several hundred units and sails through a test looking for things under 47 cm, while every sprite in it is thirty centimetres - three orders apart. The right measure for a flat is the SPRITE, which the batch already carries as `size`; this is MAC1's own argument applied to the pass that never got it. FOUR TEXELS is what confines the change: against each cascade's texel it is 4.7 cm, 19 cm and 94 cm, so the near two land BELOW the existing 0.5 floor and cannot move, and only the far cascade stops carrying anything under about a metre. The lantern replays pass no texel and are untouched, which is right rather than convenient - a cube face is 512 over an 18-unit range, so four of its texels is under the floor anyway. F5's batch line is RETIRED with it, because once the sprite test exists F5 cannot decide anything about a flat (a flat's radius is hypot(w,h)/2, so it fired only where h < 4 texels already held) - and how that was found is the useful part: F5's behavioural pin kept passing for the WRONG reason and its mutant survived, which is what said so. 4 pins, 8 mutants (8 dead), six bugs5 records re-aimed and one retired. **The lesson: a cull that measures the wrong extent is not a weak cull, it is no cull at all - and it sits there for months looking like one, because the code that would have caught it is the code it is standing in for.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - TREES1 2026-09-19, Mac ("There's this weird darkening effect happening to trees"): PERF-SUN1 MEETING A SPRITE, and the argument that produced it was HALF RIGHT. The cheap far tap rests on an ANTIALIASING claim - each tap is already a hardware 2x2, the far texel is ~2 pixels at 100 m, so the other eight soften nothing anyone can resolve - and that holds perfectly for the terrain, meshes, rigs and water, every one of which shades PER FRAGMENT and is smoothed by its own neighbours. A FLAT is not like that: EL_BB_FS reads the sun map ONCE at the sprite's base and wears that value over the whole quad (EL2's own decision - a sprite sampled at its own fragment would shadow itself), so the kernel there is not softening an edge, it is the only gradation the tree has. One tap makes a tree whose foot sits near a shadow edge flip fully lit to fully dark, the whole sprite at once, and jump again at the cascade boundary as you walk toward it. `sunShadowSoftAt` keeps the kernel at every distance and the flat is its only caller; the saving stands almost entirely, because the ground is where the fragments are. The pin asks the CALL SITES rather than the shader text, since every one of these shaders pastes SHADOW_GLSL and contains both names. 5 mutants, all dead; EL2's flat pin re-aimed by content. **The lesson: the optimisation was correct about the pixels and wrong about one caller, because that caller does not have pixels in the sense the argument assumed - "below the resolution of a pixel" means nothing to a surface that takes one sample for ten thousand of them.**
- `01-Overview/Audit-Perf-Sun.md` - AUDIT PERF-SUN/PERF-FOG 2026-09-19, Mac ("Lets do an audit on everythinf"): FOUR FINDINGS, ALL FIXED - AND TWO OF THEM WERE THE PINS LYING RATHER THAN THE CODE. **F4** (pre-existing, the one live bug, and not from this slice): `_c3` decodes into a scratch its caller names, and the billboard-tint site named `_decB` TWICE - so `mc` and `sc` were the same Float32Array, `sc`'s decode overwrote `mc`'s contents, and the next statement built uBBTint from a "moon colour" holding the sun's. Every flat in the world, every night. It is the only site in the file that holds two decoded colours live, which is why it is the only one that could have it; it surfaced while auditing `_fogColorLinear`, which PERF-FOG had just given its OWN triple against that exact hazard, and it had been quoted verbatim inside an EL1 mutant record all along - which is how a defect hides inside a law nobody reads as a claim about correctness. The pin holds the general rule too: no statement may take two decodes into one scratch. **F3**: the water pin proved `diff` has one consumer, and `diff` is not the variable the gate touches - `shadow` is, and it has TWO (the diffuse term and a sun specular). Still output-identical, now for a stated reason (every consumer carries uSunScale itself) rather than a lucky one; and the pin strips TRAILING comments before counting, because the water's own `#version` line ends in "sun shadow". **F2**: the SHADOW_PCF_CASCADES note claimed a cascade count the constant does not cover would keep the kernel - the test is `c >= N`, so every cascade OUTWARD is cheap. The code is right (the cascades ascend, so further is always coarser); the note was false, which is this slice's own lesson turned on itself. **F1**: the sway door's `_swaySearch` was declared below its reader - a TDZ shape this port has already lost a boot to once. REFUTED with numbers: the cascade 1/2 seam gets LESS visible (the far side is sharper than it was - 47 cm against 94 - so the step falls from 5x to 2.5x), and the classic skin pays nothing. RECORDED, NOT TAKEN: cascade 1's texel is ~1.5 pixels at 30 m, the same case arithmetically, kept at 3x3 because there the kernel is a soft EDGE and not only antialiasing. 8 pins, 20 mutants (20 dead). **The lesson: a pin that passes for the wrong reason is worse than no pin, because it is also a claim that somebody checked.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - PERF-FOG 2026-09-19, Mac ("keep looking for improvements"): A UNIFORM WAS BEING DECODED ONCE A FRAGMENT. `elFinish` is the lane's output and runs in EVERY lane shader there is - the terrain, the meshes, the rigs and every flat in the world - and it opened with `mix(elDecode(uFogColor), tm, ...)`. `elDecode` is the piecewise sRGB curve: three `pow()` calls, on a UNIFORM, identical for every pixel of the frame and recomputed for every one of them. GLSL has no per-draw stage between a uniform and a fragment, so the only place it can be computed once is the host: `uFogColorLin` is declared in the shared block (a fifth lane shader cannot be written without it) and uploaded only to a program that asked - a classic program does not declare it, a lane program that never calls elFinish has it optimised out, both read null and skip. THE LAW IS NOT RESTATED: the lane already carries the decoder the shader compiles (`decode3`), so the renderer asks the lane it was handed rather than keeping a second copy of the sRGB constants, and the two are held against each other by reading the four numbers back OUT of the shader text. The cache is keyed on the display triple, starts at NaN (a zero triple would match a legitimately black fog and never recompute), owns its own scratch rather than `_c3`'s, and is invalidated on a lane swap - a cache keyed on its input alone cannot see that the FUNCTION changed. The failure this could not be allowed to have is a BLACK FOG: a uniform the optimiser drops reads null, the upload skips, elFinish mixes toward black, and it compiles clean - so the probe LINKS all four lane programs in a real driver and asks for the location. Also corrected here, same day: PERF-SUN's recorded remedy for the >1000 draws was WRONG - spatial clustering trades draw calls away to buy vertex work, and one-per-texture is already the minimum; the recount names the per-pixel FLAT batches as the largest source and the two real remedies as projects with a real trade. 4 pins, 11 mutants (11 dead), EL1's fog pin and mutant re-aimed by content. **The lesson: a shader is the one place where "it's just a constant" costs you two million times a frame.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - PERF-SUN 2026-09-19, Mac ("exterior shadows at a distance, tree sway at a distance ... over 1000 calls and looking up in the sky restores frame rate"): LOOKING UP IS THE DIAGNOSIS. The sun cascades are built around the EYE, not the view direction, and each shadow replay culls by its own cascade's frustum - none of that changes when the camera tilts, and neither does the air pass or the sim. The one thing that collapses when you look at the sky is the number of shaded FRAGMENTS, so the >1000 draw calls, whatever else they cost, are not what the sky gives back. **PERF-SUN1**: `sunShadowAt` filtered 3x3 in every cascade, and each of those nine samples is ALREADY a 2x2 (the sun map is COMPARE_REF_TO_TEXTURE with LINEAR filtering, so one `texture()` is a hardware bilinear PCF) - an effective 4x4. Worth it at cascade 0's 1.2 cm texel, which is EL7's contact hairline; not at the FAR cascade's 23 cm, which is about two pixels at a hundred metres, over most of an outdoor screen because cascade 2 is everything past 48 units. The near two keep the kernel; the far one returns on one tap. **PERF-SUN2**: the sun term was one flat product, and GLSL evaluates every operand of one - a surface facing AWAY from the sun paid nine hardware-PCF compares and a cloud sample and multiplied them by the zero in front of them. Every north-facing wall, every back slope, and the whole world whenever the sun is low. `diff` reaches the light exactly once, so gating it is output-identical rather than an approximation; the `uSunScale` half is a uniform branch that takes out dusk, dawn and the night, and a FLAT's gate is `uBBSun`, which stops every sprite in the world reading the sun map after dark. Verified in a real WebGL2 driver (`tools/perfSunShaderProbe.mjs`), not asserted. **The tree sway is CLEARED**: the lean is baked per BATCH at build and the host uploads one wind vector a frame for every flat there is - though the door beside it was parsing the query string once a frame, and is read once now. **RECORDED, NOT FIXED**: the >1000 draws are PERF4's merged static batch, one sub-mesh per texture per pixel; per-sub-mesh culling cannot help because a merged sub-mesh spans the whole 819-unit pixel, and the fix is to merge per texture AND per spatial cluster, which is its own slice. 4 pins, 15 mutants (15 dead), two older records re-aimed by content. **The lesson: "over 1000 calls" named the thing that was easiest to count, and the sky named the thing that was actually being paid - a product in a shader is not a series of conditions, it is a promise to evaluate all of them.**
- `01-Overview/Audit-Hearth.md` - AUDIT HEARTH1 2026-09-19, Mac ("audit this"): FOUR FINDINGS OVER THE WORLD'S OWN FIRES, ALL FOUR FIXED - and two of them were a sentence the code told me about itself. **F1**: the hearths ignored the mod's switch. `camps.js` never needed one, because a camp has to be PLACED - with Climates & Calories off the pool is empty and answers no by itself, so the gate was implicit in the data. A brazier is in the world either way, so the moment `byFire` read a pool nobody placed, that gate stopped holding and nothing replaced it: a fire bowl answered the activation ray with a cooking list and reported `byFire` to a law nobody had turned on. **F2**: `byFire` runs EVERY FRAME - the player ticker calls its host's `survivalEnv` per frame and decides internally whether a minute rolled - and both halves of it were built for a cadence nobody had checked. It asked `nearestHearth`, which cannot stop early, for a question whose answer is a yes (a city's list walked end to end sixty times a second); and the streaming host minted an array and an object per fire per frame, the exact churn PERF-LIGHTS removed from the lanterns in the same file, because a comment I wrote said it ran on the survival tick and therefore needed no pool. The sentence was sound and its premise was false. **F3**: the three hosts measure a hearth's height differently (the exterior pair take the TOP of the flat, the interior the centre plus a per-record offset, a dungeon flat's stored y IS its centre) - each right about where the LIGHT is, none of it worth normalising, and nothing had said so while the activation box was sized as though one convention held everywhere. **F4**: `collectHearths` was exported and called by nothing but its own test, beside three hand-written copies of the test it performs. Refuted: the cached interior restore (the fill precedes it), the online dimension (a hearth is world geometry with no record to publish), and that the interior pool could stand a camp. 15 pins, 33 mutants (33 dead). **The lesson: a comment that justifies an omission is a claim, and it ages exactly as badly as the code it sits over.**
- `06-Systems/Climates-Calories.md` - HEARTH1 2026-09-19, Mac ("Does this version of C&C not let you use braziers as extra campfires to cook from?"): IT DID NOT, AND THAT WAS A GAP RATHER THAN A RULE. `byFire` looked at ONE pool - camps somebody had PLACED - so every brazier, fire bowl and tavern hearth in Daggerfall was a sprite and a point light: a player standing over a roaring brazier was as cold, as wet and as roughly rested as one in a field, and had to burn a Campfire Kit two feet from it to cook a fish. `survival/hearth.js` is the law and it is mostly its own EXCLUSIONS - three archive-210 records are a fire you can stand over (0 bowl with fire, 1 the flame a camp itself stands, 20 the brazier torch) and everything else in the lights archive is out: the candles, lanterns and chandeliers obviously, the eleven records DFU's own AddLight switch leaves unnamed, and the two WALL TORCHES, which are a real flame excluded because they are bracketed at head height and counting them would make every lit corridor a kitchen. All FOUR HOSTS collect off walks they already do (the exterior pair split theirs out of the lantern list, which carries the texture record now; the dungeon reads its own flats because its lights are RDB Light RESOURCES with no texture; the interior takes its context's list), and the streaming host's ride the floating origin. A hearth IS `byFire` - the warmth, the drying and SURV4's camp-grade sleep - and a ray target that opens the cooking list. It is NOT a camp: `campAt` still answers with camps alone, because the menu, the packing and the online record key on a record a brazier does not have. The INTERIOR's `byFire` had been a hard FALSE, right while camps were the only fire and camping indoors is refused, wrong the moment the room's hearth counts; it carries a pool now that can never stand anything and exists for two answers. 11 pins, 24 mutants (24 dead). **The lesson: the gap was not in the rule but in the pool it read - one fire had been made the only fire because it was the only one anything had been asked to look at.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - GHOST1 2026-09-19, #bug-reports (Clerical Error: "loaded from a save and we have ghost campfires now"; kurkku: "sprites disappear and reappear at certain(?) angles"): TWO REPORTS, ONE BUG, IN THE CULL SHIPPED THAT MORNING. `frustumPlanes` returns UNNORMALISED planes on purpose - `aabbOutside` only reads a sign - but `sphereInPlanes` compares `dot + d < -r`, which is a distance against a radius only when the normal is unit length, which is why `spherePlanes` sits beside it and why every older sphere cull goes through it. PERF-CROWD and PERF-CROWD2 both reached past it: on a 60-degree frustum the side planes carry |n| = 1.4 and the top and bottom 2.0, so a sprite's radius counted for as little as HALF of itself and the cull ate a band around the frustum edge - widest close to the eye, which is where you stand at a campfire, and at the screen edge, which is what turning does. Swept over ~440,000 placements whose quad really lands in the clip box, the raw planes throw some away and the normalised ones throw away none. AND THE LIFT WAS IN THE WRONG PLACE: both culls hand-rolled the bottom-anchor half-height while `batchVisible` - the copy the shadow replay and the AIR PASS's emission replay use - had none, so the main pass dropped a flat the emitters kept and what was left was the BLOOM of a sprite that never drew. That is the ghost, literally. The lift lives in `batchVisible` now and both copies delegate; a negative height (the upside-down torch flame) lifts downward with its quad. EV3's box tests take the normalised planes too, identical over 10,000 boxes because a positive divisor cannot move a sign. 6 pins, 10 mutants (10 dead), 4 perfon2 records retired into their new home. **The lesson: a helper that exists BECAUSE the other one is wrong for your case is not interchangeable with it - and when two passes cull one object by two copies of a rule, the bug does not hide, it draws.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - GRAIN1 2026-09-19, Mac ("distance terrian has a weird grain look", and before asking: "im not sure if we can tackle this without taking a performance hit"): IT COSTS NOTHING AND MAY GIVE SOME BACK. The grain is minification aliasing - the tile array was MIN_FILTER NEAREST with no mipmap, so a distant pixel covering a dozen texels picked one of them and a different one each time the camera drifted. A mipmap is the only cure, and the reason there was none was not an oversight: the shaders sample `fract(unwrapped)`, which jumps a whole tile at every edge, and `texture()` picks its mip from the derivative of the coordinate it is HANDED - so turning mipmapping on naively draws a blurred line around all 16,384 tiles of every streamed pixel, far worse than the grain. `unwrapped` does not jump; its derivative rotated by the tile's own ROT is the true footprint, and `textureGrad` takes it directly. One sample either way. Both terrain shaders and the water pass (same array, same wrap) take it; the chain is built, minification is trilinear, and MAGNIFICATION IS LEFT AT NEAREST so the ground underfoot keeps its square texels. Anisotropy capped at 4 where the driver has it, optional. THE COST, honestly: the mipmap REDUCES texture bandwidth at distance (unmipped minification is the worst case for a texture cache), memory is +33% of a sub-megabyte array, and the frame this ships into is CPU-bound anyway. Verified in real WebGL2 rather than asserted. 4 pins, 10 mutants (10 dead). **The lesson: "we cannot filter this" was true of the sampler it was written against and had been carried as a property of the terrain ever since - the wrap was never the obstacle, handing the wrapped coordinate to the hardware was.**
- `01-Overview/Audit-Online-Perf.md` - AUDIT ONLINE-PERF 2026-09-19, Mac ("Lets audit this before merging"): THE LARGEST CHANGE IN THE BRANCH INTRODUCED A SILENT CORRECTNESS BUG AND NO PIN IN THE TREE COULD SEE IT. **F1**: PERF-CROWD2's cull skipped `keyOf` for every batch it culled - but `keyOf` is not that pass's private bookkeeping. The shadow replay and the air pass's emitters both read `b._bbKey`, and both take it as it stands (`?? recompute` fires only when it is ABSENT, never when it is STALE), so a batch culled from the camera kept the key it had when it was last on screen. A mobile animates by writing its RECORD (MAC4), and the cascades reach 240 units - off screen is exactly where those casters live - so an off-screen foe would cast the silhouette of whatever frame it was on when it left the view, or none at all once that texture was gone. Found by asking what else reads the thing the cull skips; 9,000 tests could not, because the key is only ever observed through a texture lookup two passes away. **F2** (pre-existing, fixed): a per-light range array shorter than the light list read `undefined`, which lands in a Float32Array as NaN, and a NaN far plane goes on to the point-shadow matrices and the shader's depth reconstruction - a cliff with no edge marked, since the host sizes its animator at 4096 and nothing checks against it. **F3/F4** recorded: the lantern pool never shrinks (a hundred kilobytes against a pixel's megabytes, and shrinking it costs the re-mint PERF-LIGHTS exists to remove), and the shadow replay still culls by the unlifted sphere (a clipped shadow is not a vanished head, and changing it moves EL5's pinned counts). 12 pins, 31 mutants (31 dead). **The lesson: every fix in this branch was a removal, and F1 is what removal costs - a cull is not free just because the thing it skips is invisible.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - PERF-CROWD2 2026-09-19 ("Continue"): THE SAME ONE-LINE OMISSION IN EIGHT PLACES IS ONE BUG IN THE WRONG LAYER. PERF-ON2 culled the peers, PERF-CROWD the town beside them, and then the same uncut submission turned up in every other host - the dungeon's mobiles/drops/spells, the interior's flats, the fixed city's townspeople, the spell missiles, and worldModes' FIVE separate calls for blood, torches, drops, foes and guards. Seven call sites and an eighth waiting to be written. `drawBillboards` takes the test now, so every host is correct by construction. It culls AFTER the shadow record on purpose - everything still CASTS, only the drawing is culled, so nothing goes dark because its caster stepped off screen - and the planes are recomputed once a CALL rather than cached on the frame stamp, because the panel bracket swaps proj/view without bumping it. The sphere is the batch's own, lifted half a height for the bottom anchor. `?cull=off` turns it off with everything else and `stats.bbCulled` says how many the frame skipped. 11 pins, 28 mutants (28 dead).
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - PERF-CROWD + PERF-BASIS 2026-09-19 ("Continue with fixes"): THE TOWN WAS NEVER CULLED. **PERF-CROWD**: PERF-ON2 culled the peers, and they were not the only list the host hands the renderer by hand - `livePersonBatches` is the townspeople, the city watch, the exterior foes, the ground piles, the blow effects, the dropped torches and the camps, and not one of them was frustum-tested. Every townsman behind the camera was a draw, two texture binds and its uniforms every frame, and in a town that list is most of the frame's billboards; the world's own flats have had the test since EV3. Both lists take the same test now. THE SPHERE HAD TO BE LIFTED and that is the whole correctness of it: the stored sphere is centred on the placement point, the billboard VS is bottom-anchored, and at w 1 h 3 the radius is 1.58 against a head at 3.0 - culling by it would clip heads at the screen edge. **PERF-BASIS**: inside the shadow replay, `right` and `up` went up as uniforms once a FLAT, and only one of the four cases varies - `up` is a constant and `right` is the frame's sun basis, fixed before the cascade loop, unless a lantern's flats are turning to face it. Two uploads a flat for two numbers that could not change, three cascades deep, every frame. Hoisted to once a record, the lantern arm kept, and the texture bind now skips its repeats as the main pass's has since PERF3. 10 pins, 22 mutants (22 dead). **The campaign found a hole that predated the change: nothing in the suite could fail a mutant that stopped a lantern's flats turning to face it.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - PERF-FLICKER + PERF-LIGHTS 2026-09-19, Mac ("I don't want more tests, I want actual performance fixes"): TWO COSTS THE NIGHT WAS PAYING FOR NOTHING. **PERF-FLICKER**: EL8 spends the point casters on a schedule - the nearest two redraw their six cube faces every frame, the rest every third - and a slot also redraws when its light CHANGED, which is right. But the change test compared the light's RANGE, and a lantern's range is ANIMATED: CityLightAnimator wanders it inside a one-unit band fourteen times a second. So every caster rebuilt all six faces every frame and the whole schedule was dead - thirty-six face replays a frame instead of twenty, each a full replay of the casters in that lantern's reach. The cube's far plane is the range rounded UP to a quantum now, and the matrices, the change test and pointParams all take that value (they must agree - the fragment stage reconstructs depth from P.w), so a flicker cannot move it and rounding UP means no shadow is ever clipped short. **PERF-LIGHTS**: the night branch built an array and an object PER LANTERN every frame, plus a translation triple per pixel, all thrown away the moment nearestLights had picked its sixteen - hundreds of them, sixty times a second, in a frame the readout says is script-bound. A pool, refilled in place, with the live count riding into the selector as a trailing argument whose default keeps every other caller's meaning; the selection is held identical by equivalence over 60 random towns. 8 pins, 19 mutants (19 dead). **The lesson: EL8's schedule and the lantern flicker were each correct, and the pair was not - a cache key that includes an animated value is not a cache, and nothing in either file could see the other.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering-Arc.md` - PERF-ON2 + PERF-CPU 2026-09-19, Mac ("Online mode needs further performance improvements", with a readout: 51 fps, frame 19.7 ms, SCRIPT 23.3 ms, draws 1365, binds 820): THE FRAME IS CPU-BOUND AND EVERY INSTRUMENT MEASURED THE GPU. Measured before anything was changed, and two fixes died of it: the net layer's per-frame allocation churn costs **0.04 ms at a hundred peers** and is not worth touching, and Chromium refutes the name-layer fix outright - moving the labels from `left`/`top` to `transform` buys nothing while the per-frame `font-size` dirties layout by itself, and moving BOTH takes the layer off the layout path entirely (600 layouts to 0) for 0.12 ms a frame at 72 names, which is recorded and not taken. What the measurement DID find: **the peers were never culled.** Every world flat gets a frustum test against its own box (EV3); a peer got none, so one behind the camera - or at the far edge of the relay's 2,500-unit range - was a draw, two texture binds and its uniforms every frame, 6.3 GL calls a peer measured under PERF-ON's own recording Proxy, and every one of them is the script time the readout says is gone. They are culled now, with the billboard shader's own box. And `?perf=cpu` tiles the frame on the main thread's clock - the same `mark()` call sites, no extension, plus `markCpu` so a host can mark a phase that issues no GL at all - because a script-bound frame could otherwise be investigated only by guessing. 6 pins, 13 mutants (13 dead). **The lesson: PERF-ON took the online name pass from 153 GL calls a name to 14 on 15 September; NAME1 moved the face players actually see to a DOM layer twenty-six hours later. The measured win is real and it is on a path players do not take - and nobody re-measured, because there was no instrument that could.**
- `01-Overview/Audit-Grass-Water.md` - AUDIT GRASS-WATER 2026-09-19, Mac ("audit before merging"): THE CAMPAIGN FOUND MORE THAN THE READING DID. Six findings over the four-bug slice, four fixed and two recorded. **F1**: PERF10's slot bound swept 8x8 offsets and answered 392 where a 240x240 brute force answers 394 - the peak sits at offset (0,6), x exactly ON a cell boundary where two columns are at distance zero and the row count jumps, and a coarse sweep steps over it. The square window it replaced was exactly tight, so this was a regression, and `update` had no recovery: an empty free list broke the loop and left a 30 m hole for as long as the eye stood there. Not reachable by any walk tried (max 379 of 392), so latent. Fixed at both ends - a 240-step sweep that costs less than the coarse one did (the inner loop is O(columns+rows) now), and an EVICTION so the invariant is "the field holds the nearest `slots` cells" rather than "the bound is right". **F2** (recorded, measured not taken, GRASS4-style): the path mask is one byte a tile where one bit would do - 2.6 MB at maximum land view against 330 KB - but a pixel already carries 66 KB of samples, so it is 4% of what is there, and a bitset buys 2.3 MB for an off-by-one risk in a per-blade read. **F3** (recorded, pre-existing): a town's own streets carry no mask. **F4** (recorded, strengthening): PERF11 also gives the two owner sweeps ONE liveness answer, which is what AUDIT WORLD6b-ii C2 asked for. **F5/F6**: a return value mis-described since GR5, and my own comment claiming record 9 "is water art" when this container has no ARENA2 and has looked at no texel of it - now it says where record 9 SITS, which the tree can check, and says what being wrong in the other direction looks like. 13 pins, 21 mutants (20 dead, 1 recorded equivalent). **The lesson: five of twenty-one mutants survived the first run, and three of those five were pins matching a string that also appears elsewhere in the same file - each written in the same sitting as the code it guards, by the one reader a pin is not written for.**
- `07-Rendering/Water-Arc.md` - WATER-DRAW1 2026-09-19: THE DRAW AND THE FEET ARE NOT THE SAME QUESTION. Mac's screenshot is a town pond in which every tile shimmers except one, sitting there as a flat blue square with a muddy bed showing through. The pass's shader discards a tile whose corner mask is zero, and the zero came from the table's whole-tile half being `PlayerMotor.OnShallowWaterTile` - a list DFU uses for the PLAYER'S FEET. DFU never drew a water surface, so that list was never a drawing list; WATER1 made it one and inherited its omissions. Record 9 is the omission that shows. Two tables now: `WATER_MASK_TABLE` unchanged as the LAW's (the feet and the town's navigation keep DFU's answers verbatim - adding a record there would change the physics to fix a picture) and `WATER_DRAW_MASK_TABLE` as the PASS's, with a pin holding that `SHALLOW_DRAWN` is the WHOLE of the difference. Said plainly: the list is read off a screenshot, not off ARENA2, which this container does not have - `window.__tileHere()` now prints the record under the player and both answers, so the next wrong tile reports itself as a number. The report's second half - "sometimes water tiles will be on their own as 1 tile" - is NOT a bug: it is DFU's own 0xFF-sentinel quirk, recorded at terrainSurface.js:48-51, and Mac's own guess about it was right. 2 pins. **The lesson: a list borrowed from the reference carries the reference's PURPOSE with it, and this one had been doing a job DFU never wrote it for.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering.md` + `03-World/Roads.md` - GRASS-PATH1 / GRASS-WET1 / PERF10 / PERF11 2026-09-19: THE PLACER WAS ASKING THE WRONG THING, AND THE FRAME WAS PAYING TO DECIDE NOTHING. Mac reported four bugs together. **GRASS-PATH1 + GRASS-WET1** are one fault: the placer keeps a blade by tile RECORD, and the record cannot say "path" or "wet". A track across grass writes 10/11/12/51 - the very records the natural dirt-grass ring writes - so excluding them by number would have stripped the grass off every field edge in the world to clear one path; and the water-grass shore records a stream writes (20-22, 49) are mostly-grass by texel, so blades grew straight up out of ponds, which is what "textures not taking the water tile" looks like from the ground. Each question now goes to the thing that owns it: the road painter MARKS the tiles it writes (`opts.paths`, one byte at the moment of the write - it is the only thing that knows), and the wet question goes to `waterCorners.js`, the table the water pass and the player's feet already share. **PERF10**: the grass field filled a SQUARE and the draw culls a DISC, so 92 of 484 cells were placed, packed and uploaded to be skipped every frame of their life (392 slots now, 8.6 MB less held); the `live` map's string keys were split and boxed for every live cell every frame; and the host built the near-pixel index every frame whether or not a cell was filled - lazy now, so a standing field allocates nothing. **PERF11**: the online frame built the peer-owner list and its Set twice a frame for one answer. 9 pins. **The lesson: the tile record was never the question - it answers "what does this look like", and two callers had been reading that as "is it a path" and "is it dry" for as long as the field has existed.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering.md` - GRASS5 + STREAM1 2026-09-19: THE TWO CAPS, BOTH LIFTED. **GRASS5** packed a blade from twelve floats to sixteen bytes (u16 cell-local x/z/rootY/height, u8 lean/tint/width, u8 ground+phase, the cell's origin and ground span on per-slot uniforms). The range was capped by STORAGE, not by the frame - 106 MB at 250 m - and a 300 m window packed is now smaller than the lab's own 200 m window unpacked. Range 200 -> 300. Two bugs the probe caught and no pin could, because the pins run on a fake GL that draws nothing: deriving the cell origin with floor(minX/cell) named the cell NEXT DOOR (a blade clusters up to 0.55 outside its own cell) and threw every slot 30 m out, 79 lit pixels instead of 56,000; and `_point` re-pointed the attributes with the old FLOAT shape every slot, which un-packed every lane and took the draw to INVALID_OPERATION. **STREAM1** found the crossing hitch: `buildTerrainGrid` at stride 1 is ~3 ms of main thread and a map-pixel crossing promoted FIVE pixels inline - ~15 ms in one frame, a dropped frame every 819 units walked, at EVERY land view distance, because the ring boundary is Chebyshev 3 and not the radius. Promotions queue and the frame loop spends one a frame; demotions stay inline. 5 pins, 15 mutants. **The lesson: the range was never really about the frame, and the crossing hitch was never really about the draw distance - both were believed for months because nobody had measured which half of the work was which.**
- `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` - LV2 2026-09-19, Mac ("Next up, I want to implement a new element. The enhanced level up notification"; asked what it should do about the window that opened itself, "Notify, then you choose"): THE RISING, and the end of a full-screen window arriving mid-swing. DFU posts the sheet the instant the skill sum crosses a threshold (RaiseSkills :1414) and the CLASSIC skin still does; the enhanced one announces on a HUD strip, plays the fanfare where the level was EARNED, and leaves `readyToLevelUp` set so the constellation window comes when the player asks the sheet for it. Two halves kept apart on purpose: an announcement is an EVENT and expires, a reminder is a STATE and is drawn from the live flag, so a level spent by any road at all takes it with it. The skill raises and the mastery moved onto the same strip (its box carries news and no choice, which made it the same interruption); the fanfares stay in both lanes. All four hosts announce through one seam and hand it their own `open` thunk. 16 pins, 18 mutants (17 dead, 1 recorded equivalent), probe 121/121. **The lesson: a test that cannot reach the arm it is about will pass forever - the window-opens-itself mutant survived because node has no `document` and every call took the classic lane.**
- `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` - LV1 2026-09-18, Mac ("the next enhanced UI window... our gold standard is a replication of the skyrim level up UI in our own constellation vision"): THE ASCENSION, the enhanced skin's level-up window - the last screen the character-sheet door still drew classic (not the last classic-only window in the game: the bank, the makers, the services and the automap are all still native in both skins - the arc page carries that correction), and the only screen a player is GIVEN rather than opening. The eight attributes as ONE ASTERISM on the skin's own procedural night (`ui/pixelGround.js`), a point lighting a star; the crown, the plate, the skill ribbon and the three vitals in the reference's anatomy; and Daggerfall's law underneath rather than Skyrim's - a POOL over EIGHT attributes, not one of three. It holds no law at all: it is a second FACE on whichever rollout screen `ui/charSheetDoor.js` built (classic, the Oghma's, or ORL1's mod window) and every press goes back through that screen's own `input`, so a11's Level++ and the mod's caps stay where they live. The ribbon says what Daggerfall never says - which of your skills count toward the next level - pinned by reconstructing `levelUpSkillSum` out of it over 200 generated careers. THE FOUR HOSTS RULE caught its own instance on the way: `worldModes.js` reached for a level-up screen without the door in two places, so three hosts would have worn the new face and the interior host would not. 22 pins, 14 mutants all dead, and `tools/levelUpProbe.mjs` 76/76 in a real browser with no ARENA2. LV1b closed the audit's two recorded holes: the window's chunk warms at boot and a cold fetch draws a wait instead of a blank paused game, and the chronicle finally closes on the key it is named after. **The lesson: a refusal has to be REACHABLE - `disabled` and then `aria-disabled` each made the one control on the screen unpressable, so the reason it was refusing could never be read.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering.md` - GRASS4 2026-09-18: THE GRASS PLACER'S COST WAS A STRING. Opened as a sweep of the exterior frame for the same defect GRASS2 found - work submitted whose output is discarded - and the exterior turned out to be well swept already (flats culled by ring and frustum at MAC1/EV3, terrain by pixel, cascades by texel radius at EL8, lanterns gated to night, AO at half res and bloom at a quarter). The one left was in the grass PLACER, on the CPU: `pieceIndex` is asked once per blade candidate - six thousand a cell, two cells a frame while walking - and built a template string for every one, twelve thousand a frame, each hashed, looked up and thrown away. A numeric key took the placer from 1.22 ms a cell to 0.40, 67% off, with byte-identical output. **The lesson: I also 'fixed' ground() repeating keep()'s lookup, measured it, and found it saved NOTHING once the key was a number - the duplicate was only expensive because of the string. That change was reverted rather than shipped, and world.js now carries a comment saying why, because the next reader will spot the duplicate too.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering.md` - GRASS3 2026-09-18: THE BLADES WERE NOT STANDING ON THE GROUND. Prototyped to answer whether a GPU-placed grass field can work at all - a derived blade has no baked root height, so a vertex shader must reproduce the drawn surface or every blade floats. It can, to 2.4e-4 world units against a blade of 0.5 (tools/grassHeightProbe.mjs). The prototype found something else on the way: the terrain is TRIANGLES and the placer read a BILINEAR patch over the same samples, which is a different surface. FIRST REPORTED AS A BIG BUG AND IT IS NOT: the terrain those figures came from was a 311% grade, and across real grades of 7-75% the two surfaces are 0.003 to 0.08 world units apart, with no blade off by a sixth of its height. Correctness, not a rescue. `surfaceHeightAt` is the drawn surface's own law now, kept beside the grid and the indices it has to agree with, and pinned against the REAL mesh rather than against a restatement of the arithmetic. 3 pins, 6 mutants. **Two lessons. Asking 'can a shader match the ground' forces you to say what the ground IS, which is how the bilinear read surfaced at all. And a measurement is only as good as the fixture: the scary first numbers came from synthetic terrain whose SLOPE nobody had checked, and it turned out to be a cliff - the correction is pinned at both ends so it cannot drift back.**
- `07-Rendering/Rendering.md` - GRASS2 2026-09-18: THE GRASS FADE WAS PAID IN THE VERTEX SHADER. Measured before anything was changed (tools/grassFieldProbe.mjs, a real GL context over a synthetic plane - this container has no ARENA2): 8.45M vertex invocations a frame, of which the band past the fade's start was 61% and bought 2% of the lit pixels. The fade discarded blades AFTER transforming them, so a blade culled at 180 m cost what one at 5 m cost. The threshold is the blade's INDEX now rather than a hash of its phase - same distribution, but knowable to the host, which submits only the prefix that can survive - and cells past half the range bind a one-quad blade instead of five stacked ones. 8.45M to 3.54M like for like at the lab's 200 m, 58% off, with the lit-pixel count moving by 13 in 67,800 - and at the 250 m that actually ships, 4.97M, still 41% under the old field's cost at 200 m while seeing a quarter further. Height 54 to 38 and range 200 to 250 on Mac's word; the tint takes a world-space noise so the sward has patches. GR1's byte-for-byte shader law is DEPARTED FROM on the record: the lab's text plus three named edits, applied by the pin, so a fourth still fails. 12 mutants, 3 pins extended. **The lesson: the range stops at 250 because the DRAW cost stopped tracking the area and the STORAGE did not - every cell holds near-field density at 48 bytes a blade, which is 75 MB at 200 m and 169 MB at 320. Going further is a packing problem, not a shader one, and saying so beats shipping it quietly.**
- `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` - INTRO-FIELD 2026-09-18, from a field report: THE CINEMATIC'S GATE IS THE BUTTON. "Remove all the text except the begin button" - so the kicker, the title and the ornament rule come off the MARKUP and the STYLE alike rather than being hidden. The status line was the one that needed thought: it is a role=status live region and the only thing that tells a player the tap is what enables sound, so it stays a node and stays written to, but CLIPPED rather than display:none, because a display:none live region is never announced. Read aloud, never drawn. The safety it rests on is pinned: the one message a player must SEE - a score that will not load - goes to the FOOTER, which is still drawn, and no failure may be routed to the clipped line. 1 pin, 7 mutants. **The lesson: "remove the text" and "remove the element" are different instructions when the element is what a screen reader reads.**
- `10-UI/Held-Map-Arc.md` - MAP-FIELD2 2026-09-18, from a field report: THE HELD MAP WAS A POSTER. The sprite was fitted to the whole viewport and CENTRED, its painted black matte letterboxed behind it, and the enhanced skin kept its vitals on the knuckles under it. It is bottom-anchored now on the law every held thing takes; the matte is keyed off into a canvas (the `<img>` is only the loader) against a threshold measured off the file; and the vitals go through `hidesHud`, a SECOND word on `windowStack` that sits OUTSIDE hud.js's large-HUD carve-out, because DFU repaints its large hud under its own windows and a sheet held in the hands is not one. Then Mac again: "there's still a gap at the bottom of the arms" - and it could not be cropped away. Most of the forearm reaches `SPRITE_ART_FOOT` and the bite already carries it off the edge, but a minority of columns stop short, notches that burying would take the paper off the screen with. (Those column counts were the FIRST painting's; the art has been replaced twice since.) So the pixels are AUTHORED: `extendCuffs` carries each cut column's lowest solid pixel to the sprite's foot. MAP-FIELD3/4 2026-09-19, from two more field reports ("the ingame map 9n the map appears going off the edge", then a third painting: "Try this instead"): `PAPER` was WRONG, and had been on the original art too - about 10px left and 12px above the sheet's real edge, which is the ink running onto the torn border and the sky. It is the largest upright rectangle lying wholly on the parchment now, measured, with the overhang pinned at zero. And THE THUMB KEY IS A COLOUR: brightness failed on all three paintings (bronze at luma 148 against parchment at 130; then a cream middle that cuts at 154 and a burnt border that does not), so the seed is red-minus-blue - parchment is warm everywhere, steel is not - catching 36% of the thumb and 5 pixels in 340,918 of sheet, each a crack speck the flood drops as an island. Colour only seeds; shape does the rest (flood in from the side the thumb enters, CLOSE the lit ridge, FILL what is enclosed). RECORDED DEPARTURE: the black matte key and the brightness hand key are GONE - this painting carries real alpha, and its grey gauntlets reach luma 0, so a brightness key would punch holes through them; the absence is pinned by name. `extendCuffs` asks the CUFF BAND now, after two wrong tests smeared first the sheet's torn edge and then the whole parchment. MAP-FIELD5 the same day ("Can you lower it on the screen more"): `HELD_MAP_BITE` 0.03 -> 0.11, and the cuff pin re-aimed off the MARGIN and onto the LAW, since the margin is a number that moves with the bite. MAP-FIELD6 the same day ("Some of the glyphs are hard to read... more readable, without clutter and keeping the same design"): it was never CONTRAST - the pen is luma 43 on parchment at 172. A label was free to land on the next town's MARK, because placeNames tested labels against labels and nothing else - LATENT, though: this sheet has inked no names since MAP-FIELD2, so it is a fix to inkMap's law and not to anything on screen, and the first draft of this line wrongly called it the fault Mac saw; every glyph the band inks now seeds that test, and a name has four sides to try before it is dropped. Rank survives but decides the better SIDE now rather than who is named at all. Each glyph and name is laid twice - a parchment-light HALO pass, then ink - which takes clutter away rather than adding it, because the sheet's own cracks are the pen's own hue; the halo is a PASS, since per mark a neighbour's halo bites a hole in ink already drawn. And the relief carets got their own quieter tone, since a dense hatch at the pen's weight reads as ink competing with the marks. MAP-FIELD7 the same day ("when you open or close your map, I want the sprite to come in and go out at the bottom of the screen instead of fading in"): the clocks had SAID the sheet rises and lowers since MAP-FIELD2 and the code under them was a fade - it travels now, the stage carried on its OWN height (`translateY(100%)` needs no viewport number and clears the edge at every size) and eased on the port's one smoothstep. The chrome does not travel, because the top bar and card are anchored to the viewport's edges; the fade moved off the root onto the root's children, skipping the stage by identity, since the root carries the stage and fading it would fade the sprite (a CSS rule on a custom property was tried and withdrawn - the suite has sixteen fake documents with `style` as a bare object, so that law would have held only where the harness was rich enough). In the hands lane nothing slides at all - the arm brings the sheet in and a stage transform would drag the ink off the paper the rig is holding. 81 mutants, a 20-check probe. **The lesson: every constant here is a measurement, and none of these defects was visible to a pin - the harness renders nothing, so nothing that is about a PICTURE can be seen from inside it. Three claims in this work's own first draft were wrong and were corrected by the probe rather than by looking: that the gauntlets were neutral pewter, that NO sheet pixel falls under the colour line, and that the right forearm crosses under the sheet.**
- `01-Overview/Handoff-FixPackage2.md` - CLOSED 2026-09-18: MAC'S **`FixPackage2`**, **`daggerfall-loot-changes`** AND **`Lootchanges3.0`** ZIPS. The third zip paid the lesson out at once: TWO of its ten files had an author diff of ZERO and a base older than the integration hours before it, so copying them would have cleanly deleted `HUNT_LOOT_SCALE` and its test - skipped, because a file whose author diff is zero carries no intent. It landed `lootThemes.js` (an Imp can no longer hand over a Unicorn Horn: a curated per-mobileType subset of the CreatureIngredients pools, with the ODDS untouched - the roll still happens and the ladder still halves) and the Centaur counted as a humanoid. The zip shipped no test; `loot.test.js` now pins all four halves including that the odds do not move. AND **`daggerfall-loot-changes`**, 24 files, integrated. The lesson from the last hand-off held and was the whole job: the loot zip was cut from HEAD and copied in as given, but FIVE of FixPackage2's files were cut from an older tree - `world.js` from `dfd6f597`, **29 commits behind**, `worldModes.js` and `dungeonContext.js` from `65b730d9`, 31 behind - so a straight copy would have looked like a 1,143-line change to `world.js` when the author's real diff was 155, and quietly reverted the other ~1,000. Every file was 3-way merged against the base its own content identified; 13 of 15 merged clean, and the three real conflicts in `world.js` were all COMBINES, not choices (D-ONLINE2's comment kept over the zip's older phrasing; ONLINE-LOAD1's guard placed AHEAD of AUDIT-TO1 G1's travel-destination clear, because a refused load must not cancel the player's journey on its way out; SURV7's survival-clock alignment kept beside the zip's `uploadRecordFrame` dep). **LANDED: the loot rebalance** (humanoid loot-table items and worn gear to a quarter, gold untouched; animal, corpse-food and hunting yields halved on stochastic rounding, so a one-unit catch averages the cut instead of rounding back up), **ONLINE-CLASS1** (a peer without a Morrowind body stands as its class-enemy sprite; the look's new `class` key is OMITTED, not null, when there is none - SOC1's own pin names that exact mutant, and `lookKey` hashes the look, so a null would have missed every cached look and changed the bytes of every older peer's hello; `RELAY_VERSION` bumped to `world83` with its law row appended), **themed camps** (a group is seeded once and its members drawn from the seed's own faction), and **ONLINE-LOAD1**. **CAMP2 LANDED, but NOT as offered** - and this reverses a refusal recorded above, so here is what changed. It was refused for three reasons; `mobileFactions.js` arriving answers only the first. The other two were both consequences of the flag being GLOBAL: setting `suppressInfighting` on the entity skips the infighting arm of the three-arm chain entirely and drops to the arm below it, where a foe targets nothing but the player - the player's own summoned ally included - and it outlived the `campId` that justified it, since a quickload drops the id and not the flag. The exemption is now the CAMP'S: `enemyTargets.js` spares a campmate its campmates and nobody else, and it lapses with the id rather than leaving a foe that ignores the world. `enemytargets.test.js` pins all three halves, with a no-camp-id control so the pin cannot go vacuous. The zip's **`RemotePlayers` arguments** were refused last time as inert; they are read now (`_buildMobile` bails without `uploadRecordFrame`), so they land. **`isOnlineWorldSession` is still absent and still refused.** The zip also shipped JSDoc that `tsc` rejected and left `camp1_groups.test.js` un-updated against its own rewrite of `rollGroupComposition`; both fixed here, along with the contract row for `createBillboardBatch`, which said `number` where the file's own comment says the pair is `number|string` "on purpose". 1 new test, 3 mutants re-aimed by content, ~260 cites re-resolved. Gate green: 8,944 tests, 0 failures.
- `06-Systems/Climates-Calories.md` - AUDIT VC6 2026-09-18: THE CORPSE'S FOOD ROLLED ON NOBODY'S STREAM. Chased down from a city-guards pin that failed one run in eight. The three pools that raise a death handed `raiseEnemyDeath` an entity and nothing else, so SURV2's handler - which ROLLS - fell to `Math.random`, and about fifteen per cent of humanoid kills grew one or two items nobody could predict; `spawnEnemyLoot` three lines away was already taking the pool's own stream. And the luck it rolls against came from `setSurvivalPlayerReader`, WHICH HAD NO CALLER ANYWHERE IN THE TREE - every player in the game rolled at luck 50 and the mod's whole luck term was dead. The reader is deleted and every pool hands its own stream and its own player's luck. 1 pin driven through the live handler, 6 mutants. **The lesson: a flaky test is a bug report. This one had been dismissed as a flake twice, and it was telling the truth both times.**
- `07-Rendering/Volumetric-Clouds-Arc.md` - REOPENED and CLOSED 2026-09-18 (VC6, Mac, four reports in one message). **(a) THE REPEAT** - "clouds repeat pretty consistently when its partly cloudy". Two numbers: the shape volume tiles every 12288 m and the coverage field meant to break it up tiled every 13107 m, so the grids stayed in phase and the same bank came round inside ONE visible sky (the march reaches 24 km). The variation goes to 65536 m and its four channels do four jobs instead of one - two coverage frequencies, and a vector that WARPS the position before the shape is read, so the lattice arrives bent and the straight edges the eye locks onto are not there to find. No texture read was added. And the field had only ever had ONE THING TO SAY: one flatness, one ceiling, for the whole zone, so the profile gains `vary` and a scattered sky now holds both flat-bottomed decks and shallow towers. **(b) THE GOLDEN HOUR** - "clouds arent influenced by the sun". Geometry, not taste: a deck 2300 m up sees a degree and a half past the ground's horizon, and its light was being cut while the sun was still 2.5 degrees UP. Three terms in the march, all weighted by how low the sun is and all dead at noon - the sky is two colours at dusk (gold toward the sun, blue away, both hue-only so a tint can never be a brightness cheat), a low sun lights the cloud's UNDERSIDE, the direct gain opens. **(c) THE SHAFTS** - the mask was sky depth alone, so a covered sun threw full rays and a tree's canopy cut a silhouette out of a beam that should not have been there; they read the cloud shadow at the player's feet now, squared. One field, three consumers. **(d) THE FRAME** - the density function returns before its first texture read outside the band, the march strides over empty air and backs the stride out at a cloud's edge, the light march stops where it cannot be seen; and `?perf=zones` breaks the frame into spans that TILE it, because this session cannot see Mac's GPU and the tier choice should be made against numbers. 9 pins, 45 mutants, and a field probe on a real GPU (`tools/vc6ShaftProbe.mjs`, 6 checks). **THE GATE SAW NONE OF THE THREE REAL FAULTS.** `flat` is a reserved interpolation qualifier in GLSL ES 3.00, so the new gradient parameter took the sky down at boot with every pin green. The warp at three pixels FOLDED the lattice (its amplitude has to stay under its own field's feature size, not under the field it bends) and smeared a fan at the zenith - one pixel, chosen against the same shot on the tree before the change. And the shaft gate was reading a NULL deck every frame of the real game: beginFrame clears the deck, `prepare` runs inside beginFrame, and both hosts set the frame's deck after beginFrame returns - it is read at the resolve now. **THE AUDIT AFTER THE SLICE FOUND TWO MORE, BOTH MINE**: `uLightDir` is not always the sun - once the sun is down it is the MOON's, and the dusk weight was a smoothstep on it, so a moon near the horizon lit the undersides at midnight; it is a pure CPU law off the SUN's own direction and weight now, exactly 0 at night. And `hue()` on a black colour answered a BLACK tint rather than a neutral one. It also caught one of its own mutants re-aimed wrong - the replacement truncated `&& world)` and was dying of a syntax error rather than of its law. And it MEASURED what VC6d had only asserted: the stride's sky is byte-identical under cloudy and thunder, and 0.06 of 255 levels under sunny. **The lesson, three times over: a field that never repeats still reads as repetitive if it only has one thing to say; a pin that drives no frame and compiles no shader cannot tell you whether the thing works; and a uniform's NAME is not its meaning - uLightDir is the light's, and the light is not always the sun.**
