- `06-Systems/Server-Update-Notice.md` - CLOSED 2026-09-17 (Mac: "a server restart notice whenever we push server updates. Like a notice that pushes in the chat window"). **"Server" is two things and only one of them is the server:** the RELAY moved by hand then (`npx wrangler deploy`, nothing in CI - since SRV-N/CI, PR #209, `.github/workflows/relay-deploy.yml` deploys a version drift on every push to main) and when it does every socket drops at once; the CLIENT BUILD moves on every merge, several times a day, and nothing at all tells a tab held open across one that it is now running code we replaced - which is how a player reaches `systems/staleChunk.js`, a deleted hashed chunk breaking the game in their hands. Mac's words name the first; Mac's situation is the second. Both, through one presenter. `RELAY_VERSION` had named the deploy to `/health` alone since AUDIT WORLD34 D4 and NOTHING ON THE WIRE CARRIED IT; it rides both welcomes now - the channel's too, which returns early and is the only welcome a chat link ever sees, so a version on the full welcome alone would have left the chat blind to the restart that dropped it. The detector remembers in a SET, which is MANY ARMS ONE ANSWER for the third time in a week (a presence socket plus one chat socket per tab all re-welcome on a deploy, and a slot would have said it once each) and buys a second property a slot cannot have - a rollback or a half-rolled edge cannot ping-pong a notice. The two halves are deliberately NOT the same shape: the relay's version is a name never seen before, so the first is a baseline and only the second is news; the build's tag is one we already know (`BUILD_TAG` against the live page's `<meta name="build-tag">`), so it compares directly and needs no baseline. `buildTagOf` is one home now - `tools/verify-deploy.mjs` carried its own copy of the regex, and two spellings of "has the site moved?" is two answers where the one that matters is unwatched. A notice is drawn with NO NAME AND NO `#tag`, because a name is forgeable - the filter guards the words, not the impersonation - and `tagOf('')` is a real-looking hash identical on every notice; `system` is a flag that never travels on the wire, and the pin drives a relay frame that tries to set it anyway. It lands on EVERY tab: a restart did not happen to a room. It does not reload for the player, for the same reason `unloadGuard.js` does not save for them. And bumping the relay reddened NINE separate `RELAY_VERSION === 'world66'` assertions across nine suites - five on the first run and four more on the next, because their suites had already passed; each meant "my slice bumped it", a claim that expires at the next bump, and one test's NAME still said world64 over an assertion retyped twice. They ask monotonically now (`test/relayVersion.mjs`). 18 mutants, 18 killed. **THE RELAY HALF IS INERT UNTIL THE RELAY IS HAND-DEPLOYED** - the live Worker is `world66`, its welcomes carry no `v`, and a missing version is read as `unknown` and stays silent. **AUDIT-SRVN** the same day (Mac: "Audit this"): FOUR findings, and three are one omission - `v` was written as if the relay were ours. `?server=` and the menu's Relay field make it the PLAYER'S choice, which is why `wire.js` holds a law for every other welcome field and holds it at BOTH ends; `v` had a `typeof` in online.js and nothing else, and a 200 KB deploy name was accepted and held (driven). A welcome is NOT once per connection, so 5000 of them on ONE open socket pushed 5000 notices - CHAT_KEEP twenty-five times over, a player's whole chat history replaced by fake restarts - into a Set that kept every name for ever, the only unbounded accumulator the slice added beside a chat log capped at CHAT_KEEP and a `_threwKinds` capped at 32. Bounded now, FAILING TOWARDS SILENCE (an emptied set re-baselines, and a re-baseline says nothing), plus a minimum interval that is not a rate limit but a physical fact. The build poll carried two gates where one heals and the spare latches - a `_buildPolling` boolean that was redundant with the interval stamp AND could stick raised on a request that never settled, killing the poll for the session; gone, and the request is cancelled on a deadline. And `session.relayVersion` was written on every welcome and read by its own test alone - deleted, the AUDIT-CHATR `whoRows()` shape. What the audit did NOT find: the notice reaches a player, and this is genuinely not a FOUR HOSTS seam (the chat is world.js's alone by CHAT1's design) - checked rather than assumed. 14 mutants against the audit's own fixes and THE FIRST PASS KILLED ONLY 10: the bound had no assertion at all, because the flood test passes unbounded - the rate gate alone explains it - so a guard added IN THIS AUDIT was for an hour exactly the unfalsifiable line the audit existed to find. 14/14 on the second pass. **CHAT-G** closed that flag the same day on Mac's word ("Yes do it"): a chat line passes THREE gates and had only ever had two - the client gates what it SENDS (CHAT_HZ_MAX) and the relay gates what it ACCEPTS (per socket, and CHAT_ROOM_HZ_MAX for the room), and nothing counted what a client RECEIVES, so 5000 frames on one socket emptied a player's CHAT_KEEP history and refilled it with the relay's choice of text (driven). THE RATE IS DERIVED: CHAT_ROOM_HZ_MAX is what an honest relay spends on one room, so the gate admits an honest room at FULL TILT and the first frame refused is one no honest relay would have sent - gating arrivals at the sender's CHAT_HZ_MAX would have dropped real lines the moment two people talked at once, a hardening that is a chat bug. Per ROOM, because that is the unit the relay spends by and one bucket would let a loud neighbouring cell silence the room the player stands in; a room let go drops its bucket. Counted (`stats.chatsDropped`) and said on the console ONCE. 5000 frames, 20 land. 8 mutants, 8 killed.

- `01-Overview/Incident-2026-09-01.md` - CLOSED 2026-09-01, and its four **Standing lessons are binding process law** for every slice after it: (1) the palette is SHARED and mutable and any palettized-IMG load can destroy it - `incident_texture.test.js`'s site sweep is the guard, and a new palettized-IMG consumer either mints its own palette or extends that sweep; (2) a FIRST-EVER-RENDERED path is a release risk however lawful the code (winter-at-boot was correct 1:1 behaviour and still broke the live site), so any change that lights a never-drawn path gets the owner's eyes before merge - the suite cannot see pixels; (3) keep merging through GATED PRs, because deploy-and-eyeball bisects in O(log n) only when the known-good checkpoints exist; (4) never plain-merge a revert commit into the branch that carries the fix - `-s ours` or a tree-restore, since the three-way silently prefers the revert wherever the fix branch is quiet. The record itself: two texture breakages in one afternoon, both invisible to 5,600+ green tests.

- `01-Overview/Hardening.md` - THE HARDENING PROGRAM (HARD, 2026-09-14, Mac: "I really want to focus on making everything clean, hardened and not spaghetti ... I think i want to take a break of additions and do this right"). The plan and the running record for a pass that adds no features. Its principle, learned from AUDIT 66 and from `audit24_lifetimes.test.js` before it: A RULE ENFORCED BY AN ENUMERATION IS A RULE ENFORCED BY MEMORY - a gate that pins the defects an audit found protects the past, and the next instance of the same class ships green. Every slice converts a remembered rule into a DERIVED one that reads the source, finds every site the rule applies to, and fails closed on the undecided ones. HARD1 (the generative lifetime gate), HARD2 (one contract per cross-cutting system, so a law is not wired four times by hand - its first seam is the activation race), HARD3 (types at the three seams where a wrong shape crashes) and HARD4 (the vendored-work registry) are SHIPPED; HARD5 (Home.md back to an index) is written up there with its risks, and so are HARD2's remaining seams. **AUDIT-HARD (2026-09-15, Mac: "Lets audit this before merging")** read the four slices against themselves and found nine things, of which one was a defect in shipped code and three were holes in the gates. The headline: HARD1's very first fix was a DOUBLE FREE - the pool it named is a HAND-OFF, not a leak - and it stayed green because the ownership check was reading COMMENTS. Three more gates claimed to derive while quietly enumerating directories. All nine paid; the lesson recorded there is that A GATE IS CODE AND ROTS THE WAY CODE ROTS, so the gates need auditing on the same cycle as the port.
- `01-Overview/Mod-Registry.md` - THE VENDORED-WORK REGISTRY (HARD4, 2026-09-15). One row per directory under `vendor/` - what the port takes, the upstream author and version, the bundle or commit it was read from, the licence or permission, the port slice, the parity date and the bible page - plus the known deviations per row. `test/hard4_registry.test.js` derives the row list from the filesystem, so a new vendored work cannot arrive without one, and cross-checks each version and author against the vendored `.dfmod.json` so a refreshed bundle cannot drift from its row. WHAT READING THE FOURTEEN TOGETHER FOUND: SIX of them carry an unfilled `[Mac: paste the text of the permission...]` line, each sitting under a sentence that says permission WAS granted. That is a record problem and not a licence one - Mac's word with a date settles whether the port may carry the files - but the evidence was never pasted in, and one README at a time nobody would ever see that it had happened six times. Those rows read RECORD OPEN, and the gate holds the mark and the placeholder in step in BOTH directions. Also surfaced: PCAAO's port is built from the shipped 1.44 bundle decompiled, while the last single-file source in its repository is 1.40.

F2 - every worn swap re-measured the face (D32 rebuilds on equip change); memoised per identity per data generation. F3 - a miss would have been memoised for the session (the door opens before ARENA2); only a measured match is cached. 3 mutants dead. Verified: all 160 real portraits measure and agree with the eye; the parallel session's by-extension decode shares the mip shape; race spellings round-trip; never-traps holds at every link.

EL1 FOUNDATIONS SHIPPED the same day: `render/enhancedLighting.js`, the lane the renderer installs over its classic world program set (`setLightingLane`, compiled once per key and kept across swaps; the classic shaders untouched to the byte and never compiled beside the lane's on a classic page) - sRGB decode of every texel and every scene colour the renderer uploads, so lights add in linear; lanterns on a windowed inverse square (`2 / (1 + 16 x^2)` times `(1 - x^4)^2`, zero at the range) and forty-eight of them (the cap is the installed set's, read by every host's `nearestLights`); the city's and the dungeon's white lanterns given a ~1900 K flame at the host's intensity; exposure 1.4 then an extended Reinhard (white 4) that keeps a 0.12 ambient at 0.145 and blooms a torch's near field; the analytic single scattering of each lantern along the view ray, windowed to its range and scaled by the fog's own density, so the foggy dungeon shows every torch as a halo and clear air shows nothing; the fog colour blended decoded and re-encoded so a fogged fragment is the sky's colour; the far ring on the same lane and exposure. EYE ADAPTATION off the frame's mean log luminance (a 32x32 image's top mip, a 1x1 image stepped toward key/luminance: opening at 0.6/s into the dark, closing at 3/s into the light, clamped to [0.7, 1.8] so a dungeon stays dark once the eye has opened), read by every lane shader and the far ring; PROPER DARK DUNGEONS - the dungeon hosts' ambient and Better Ambience's trilight scaled to 0.35 under the lane, the interiors untouched; a Blinn-Phong glint from every lantern on the lit shaders. 6 pins; - `01-Overview/Audit-Roads.md` - SUPERSEDED before it was actioned (audit 2026-08-29, merge 2026-09-04: main had already replaced the road system whole with ROADS 22, Hazelnut's Basic Roads - the retired modules are marked on the page; the record stands). Was: COMPLETE, UNACTIONED (2026-08-29): THE ROAD DEEP AUDIT. 8 dimensions, 45 agents, 37 findings confirmed and 0 dropped at the verify stage (7 breaks / 19 degrades / 11 latent). THE LIVE DEFECT: a diagonal pixel seam SEVERS the road - the two painted bands touch at one corner point and share no edge, at 48% of all boundary crossings - which is what Mac saw as a road connected to nothing. It shipped green because the seam pin only ever drove N-S and E-W, the two orientations that cannot disagree, on blank tilemaps; and because the revert left stitchChain, the suite's only multi-pixel harness, as dead code. Confirmed sound and not to be re-audited: the router is optimal (0/546 suboptimal against an independent Dijkstra), generation is water-tight and connected over a 250-map fuzz, the bake is byte-deterministic four ways, and RG1 cannot tear a seam. Fix the diagonal seam first, writing its pin before the fix and driving it with a location on one side; then ROADS_V, one character, which silently withholds RC1 and RC3 from anyone who has already baked.
- `06-Systems/Mod-Support-Arc.md` - SUPERSEDED (2026-09-04 merge: main already carries the windmill, W2a, vendored with permission - src/world/windmillMesh.js; the page stands as the study). Was: PLANNED, not started (2026-08-29): THE THIRD ASSET-INJECTION DOMAIN. Mac's WindMills.rar study - model replacement (MeshReplacement.cs) and world-data overrides (WorldDataReplacement.cs), the two DFU injection domains the port lacks. The archive is the mod's Unity SOURCE project, not a built .dfmod, so the meshes are readable COLLADA rather than a sealed AssetBundle. Five findings settled: the watermill is 41601 not 21411 (LoadWindmill.cs is dead, absent from the manifest); the four "malformed" RMB JSONs are valid FullSerializer input (optional commas) and must NOT be repaired; the mesh axis rule is (-x, y, z) with reversed winding and the declared Z_UP is a lie (measured off the blade's mounting plank to 0.016); the mod's 21 PNGs must NOT be registered or they repaint 16 archives game-wide; and the one non-1:1 step is the two [ImportedComponent] behaviours, which are compiled C# and become named built-ins. 12 steps specified, 19 synthesized-fixture pins, the Unity prefab reader carrying the schedule risk (two shipped prefab shapes disagree structurally). First move (a) - does retail ARCH3D hold 41600/41601 - needs ARENA2_PATH and decides whether this ships as one feature or two.

AUDIT III same day found THREE port bugs that were all one mistake - state whose only observer runs somewhere the port had stopped thinking about, each behind a comment claiming faithfulness: the tone gate belongs INSIDE GetAnswerText (:1994-1995), THE %n SLOT (greetingNameNPC) is filled before the greeting record expands and emptied the instant it returns (:1136-1140) because the macro layer resolves %n through it, and THE HEADLESS SESSION must be a FIELD like C#'s npcData (:189) rather than a literal rebuilt per call - the rumor mill carried that one too, and AUDIT I had recorded its symptom as a proven equivalence, now withdrawn; the campaign's round 3 swept 170 mutants on a freshly regenerated coverage map for 147 kills, 11 gaps pinned and re-confirmed dead, 12 proven equivalents. TK-iv SHIPPED (2026-08-21): THE NPC SESSION - the module that CHOOSES the NPC the other three slices answer for. The click arms, the target chain, the faction resolution, both parent walks, GetGreetingIndex whole and TalkToNpc's three doors, plus the questor pool the Work arm draws from and the conversation save halves. Kept quirks by line: THE UNGUARDED FIRST ARM (C# writes `IsAlly(a,b) || IsAlly(b,a) && greetingIndex > 2`, and since && binds tighter the guard covers only the reversed test), THE STRANGER FLOOR (at index 8 a reputation of 6 to 29 matches neither arm and falls through to the plain reaction ladder), THE REJECTION FOUND MID-GREETING (the greeting lookup itself sets alreadyRejectedOnce, and the second test answers with the raw tokens in a box while the window never opens), THE NAMES NOBODY READS (four faction-name fields written by every arm of the ladder and read by nothing in the whole DFU tree); THE ZERO-FACTION MATCH recorded rather than emulated; 69 pins. AUDIT IV same day found THREE port bugs, two of them the mistake TK-iii's re-read had already caught once - a law left with the host that C# keeps inside the method - which is why the arc now carries it as standing law: half of TalkToNpc's tone reset was the host's, and rebuildTopicLists was shadowed by a copy nothing would ever raise. The three-round campaign (180 then 190 mutants, 0 uncovered lines both times, 68% then 87% kills) recorded a second law with it: A FIXTURE THAT CANNOT REACH THE STATE A LINE GUARDS IS NOT A TEST OF THAT LINE - every `> N` guard in the greeting ladder differs only when the index is EXACTLY N, which no single membership can arrange, so nine guards that looked thoroughly pinned were untested until six two-membership fixtures walked the index onto each bar; 16 gaps closed and 8 proven equivalents, two of them DFU's own dead stores that came back at exactly the baseline - THE BASELINE TRAP sprung and caught a third time. TK-v SHIPPING (2026-08-21): THE HOST MOUNT - the four engine modules live in the running hosts, and systems/talkMacros.js is TalkManagerMCP whole plus MacroHelper.ExpandMacros and ExpandRandomTextRecord, the reader that finally makes TK-iii's two live slots observable (%n's greeting name and %loc's map-reveal flag, both now pinned from INSIDE an actual expansion). THE QUESTOR DOOR IS OPEN: a static-NPC click carrying work opens Q4-ii's offerSocialQuest, the arm that had been waiting since the offer flow shipped. The tone gate, the reaction cache and the question counter moved off townTalk onto the engine, applying the arc's own new law to the last host still breaking it. DaggerfallInventoryWindow.EquipItem :1343-1381 verbatim, at the same seam DFU uses - the inventory WINDOW, not ItemEquipTable, so starting gear equips straight past it and a restricted class can START in gear it could never put back on. Two quirks ported: the armor MATERIAL test is gated on PLATE only - which bites on Chain2, whose 0x0103 shares a low byte with Elven - and GetWeaponSkillUsed's Skills.None = -1 masks against every bit, so an unnamed weapon-group item reads as forbidden to any restricted career. Probed live as a Mage - plate refused with a reason, dagger drawn; the probe's own first draft offered a Longsword and was rightly refused, which is now a pin) SHIPPED. + S24 SPELL ABSORPTION (2026-08-19: spellAbsorptionFlags had zero consumers, and for the SORCERER that was worse than inert - the class ships absorb=Always ALONGSIDE NoRegenSpellPoints and only the PENALTY was live, so it paid its entire classic cost and got none of the benefit it is traded for. TryAbsorption verbatim: DESTRUCTION only (DFU's own comment explains it - absorption is tested against every incoming effect, so a self-heal would otherwise be swallowed), the cost computed as if the TARGET cast it with the target multiplier and the floor of 5, and the HEADROOM refusal that is the throttle on the trait - an absorber at full magicka absorbs nothing. The two arms draw by DIFFERENT laws on purpose: classic art takes the native panel at integer scale with the 1-bit cutout and NEAREST, ours takes logoRect with blending and LINEAR, and each law is wrong for the other's art. The artwork itself then forced two RENDERER opt-ins: our logo is not classic art, and the port's screen-quad law is a 1-BIT CUTOUT - discard a<0.5, force alpha 1 - which is exactly right for classic art, because a palettized IMG IS a 1-bit cutout with index 0 transparent and every other index opaque, and exactly wrong for anti-aliased gold serifs and the dagger's soft shadow, which it would jag and cut to a silhouette; likewise NEAREST/REPEAT keeps a 320x200 IMG pixel-exact at nativePanel's integer scales and aliases a 2000px banner drawn at a non-integer one. drawScreenQuad gained a { blend } arm and uploadTexture a { smooth } arm, BOTH caller opt-ins with titleScreen.js the only caller, so no game art path changed - pinned in both directions and mutation-proven four ways. Established from the shipped bytes of the user's own MIDI.BSA and corpus-gated over the whole retail archive. THE COMMISSION WAS WRONG AND THE BYTES SAID SO: this was briefed as an XMI reader, and there is no FORM/XDIR/CAT/ XMID chunk anywhere in the file - it is HMI Sound Operating System (signature HMI-MIDISONG061595), which shares exactly one trait with XMI, note-on carrying a duration, and differs in the delta encoding, ordinary MIDI VLQs rather than interval counts. Reading it as XMI desynchronises immediately. The file is named for the format it reads. 131 songs, 1286 tracks, all decoding; the load-bearing pin is the GLOBAL GATE - with the decoded event sizes every track consumes to its end-of-track landing BYTE-EXACTLY on the next track's offset, so a single wrong size drifts and fails. getSong returns a merged tick-ordered event stream a synth can walk. Sizes proven but meanings unknown are exposed RAW and say so, and anything unrecognised throws with song and offset rather than decoding to garbage. Verified independently before merge - the chunk-signature count, the song/track totals and the offset table at 0 mismatches, and the XMI-guess mutation killing 6 pins) SHIPPED. + U22 THE OPENING VIDEO (2026-08-19: VidFile.cs ported WHOLE - the tenth format reader and a TRUE 1:1, unlike the song reader that landed beside it, because this one has a DFU source and that one has none. ANIM0001.VID now plays ahead of the menu, where DaggerfallUI.InitGame puts it: the Start window is pushed and the VidPlayer pushed ON TOP, so the splash reveals the menu when it ends. Corpus-swept over all 17 retail VIDs with two exactness gates - decoded video blocks == header FrameCount, and final stream position == file size, from 98KB to 19.7MB, so a one-byte desync anywhere could not land on the last byte. FOUR DFU QUIRKS PRESERVED rather than smoothed (a mid-stream palette desyncs the stream, frameDelay is recomputed for every block so a video block inherits the last audio delay, a swallowed exception does not advance the stream so a bad block parks the reader forever, and Audio_StartFrame consumes its data before rejecting a bad rate) and ONE mechanism deliberately changed to keep behaviour: C#'s IndexOutOfRangeException on Color32[] is emulated with an explicit bounds throw, because a JS typed array drops an out-of-range write SILENTLY and a faithful transcription would have completed the very block C# aborts - the quirk would have been lost by porting the code correctly. Audio is ported, not deferred: the clip is built verbatim and scheduled on the WebAudio clock, though the boot splash plays SILENT until something resumes an AudioContext, since a browser will not start one before a gesture. The PORTED half is SongManager.cs - the playlists verbatim (duplicates included, because a duplicate doubles that song's odds) and SelectCurrentSong arm by arm: taverns index gameDays directly so they walk in sequence day to day, dungeons seed DFRandom with unknown2 XOR (region<<8), everything else seeds on gameDays so a location's song is stable until midnight, and a list of ONE returns index 0 without consuming the generator - if it seeded, entering a temple would shift every later roll in the session. The OURS half is the synth, because DFU renders its own .mid files with a vendored synth and a SoundFont from Unity Resources: both DFU's assets, so there is nothing to port. 52 GM programs and channel-9 percussion means any sample answer ships megabytes or asks the player for an SF2, so the bank is oscillators - the only option that works the moment the page loads, and the worst sounding, so it sits behind an interface an SF2 replaces without touching the scheduler. Its specs are TASTE and are deliberately NOT pinned as truth: a pin saying 'program 48 is a sawtooth' would assert my own choices back at me, the vacuous-pin shape two audits have caught here, so the synth pins assert STRUCTURE - every GM program resolves, out-of-range clamps rather than throws, no percussion key is silent, and nothing can put a NaN in the audio graph. Music boots from the SAME seam as sound (shared.ensureAudio) for F6's reason - a second bootstrap is a second thing every host must remember - and the F6 pins were STRENGTHENED rather than relaxed to say so: one flag per subsystem, every subsystem behind the one seam. Mutation-proven eight ways. + A5b THE FM BANK AND ALL FOUR HOSTS, same day: the subtractive bank was replaced with TWO-OPERATOR FM, because that is the synthesis Daggerfall was scored for - AdLib/SoundBlaster OPL - and is why the archive carries an F*/FM* arrangement of nearly every song. Checked for a real OPL bank first and there is none: it lived in HMI's sound driver, ARENA2 has no .AD/.BNK/.OPL and no driver, and the song headers carry a device/channel map and zeros where patches would be. So the ratios stay ours on the same terms, but the METHOD is period-correct rather than arbitrary, and FM subsumes the old bank (index 0 is a bare carrier). An empty section stands and says so; the kind tag is gone because a quest is filed under its kind, not titled by it; the grouping law and the unsplit archive are unchanged, the latter because the notebook's filed header keeps only the display name) + PX21e THE LOOT WINDOW DOES NOT SCROLL (2026-08-27, Mac's call: the frame carried overflow-y itself and scrolled its own title and buttons away; it is fixed furniture now with the rows in their own box, and a long pile WIDENS to two columns rather than scrolling - as a grid, because multicol in a scroll container fragments in the block direction and scrolled anyway) + PX21f A TOOLTIP IS NOT A SCROLL BOX (2026-08-27, Mac's call: the tip inherited .packcol's overflow:auto - PX19j had turned off this element's ground and padding and missed the third - and PX21e's overflow:hidden on the loot frame was clipping the tip that lives inside it, so the frame is visible again and the flex constraint is what holds the no-scroll guarantee) + PX23 THE SPELLBOOK (2026-08-27, Mac's call off the board: four hosts built the player's book identically but for their TEXT.RSC reach, so ui/spellbookDoor.js is the fifth U52/U53 seam - and the board's 'hand-rolled duplicate' turned out to be the spell MERCHANT's shop, corrected in the record and pinned by its own deps; the enhanced book is the journal's bones a fifth time and imports every law it could read, including the lycanthropy free cast and both delete refusals in the classic's own words) + PX24 THE CHRONICLE (2026-08-27, Mac's call: the logbook and the history as ONE window, because what they hold is one subject - your notes, the messages you were sent, and where you came from; four construction sites collapse into ui/chronicleDoor.js and the CLASSIC skin keeps its two windows, since merging is an enhanced idea. Quests are deliberately absent: the pause window has carried them since PX4 and a second copy would be the two character sheets again) + U65 THE INTRO (2026-08-30, Mac's call: three splash screens over a flyover of the Iliac Bay, cut to the theme, fading into the menu - the slice main.js's own header had already named, and its constraint comes from the same sentence, since the enhanced door opens BEFORE the ARENA2 pick and the intro therefore uses NO GAME DATA: a generated province, a voxel-space ray walk over it and an orthographic view of it from above. THE GRID IS MEASURED - spectral-flux onsets and a tempo/phase fit give 127.26 BPM at phase 0.255 s, pinned against the recording's own peak times to 13 ms, with a pin that fails if those times ever stop jittering because three were first transcribed from the grid column instead. The SONG is the clock, so a stall holds the picture rather than sliding it out of step. Three beats carry three events - the launch, the kick, and the burst out of the cloud deck onto the strongest onset in the opening, where the title cuts and the whole province appears - because a move only starts on a beat if the camera is at REST when it lands. The clouds are the CUT, not decoration: the flyover's pitch is a horizon shift so its picture is always a wedge to a vanishing point, the two projections cannot be cross-faded, and a cut is invisible in a white-out. 57 pins, 53 mutants, 53 dead; nine survived their first pass and every survivor was a pin that did not pin. FLAGGED: the browser probe is written and has not been run) TI1 SHIPPED (2026-09-05): MOBILE TOUCH INPUT - the swipe is TrackMouseAttack's drag on a finger, the tap is PlayerActivate.cs:303's free-cursor ray pressed as a one-frame ActivateCenterObject so every ladder takes it unedited, a tap on a foe LOCKS ON (the camera faces its chest through the LookFilter, a dot marks it), the dial button is Tab, and twelve buttons became five; the drag is classified before a pixel reaches the attack seam because the shipped threshold is five phone pixels. TI1b (phone in hand): the speed rule ate every look-pan - a HOLD then a drag is the swipe now, and a tap on the stick's half locks too. No DFU original (DFU has no touch input) - Ledger section A. QS1-QS3 (2026-09-17, Mac: the Demon's Souls diamond): the bottom-left quickslot diamond on the enhanced HUD - main hand, off hand (shield, torch, swap-to weapon, or an empty socket), two consumable slots filled from the inventory tooltip, durability strips, counts, keybind tags with our own Xbox/PlayStation pixel glyphs, Digit1-3 the three new port actions, a slot a KIND rather than a record so it survives the save and the last bottle; Ledger A row THE QUICKSLOT DIAMOND.
